Go API

Actions and noun bindings come from a fresh demo.New() in internal/demo. Go declarations and comments come from the root ofam.dev/ochazuke package source through Go's standard go/doc library. This is a build-time snapshot, not your running application's customized registry.

03 / Exported Go API

Go API 1290

Generated exported types, functions, methods, constants and variables from non-test Go source files directly in the root ofam.dev/ochazuke package. Subpackages such as cli, cli/mcp, markdown and grid are outside this catalog's scope; command entry points under cmd/ are not library APIs. Unexported fields and function bodies are omitted. Missing prose means the declaration has no source doc comment.

Go also provides terminal documentation: run go doc -all . or go doc Runtime.Execute from the repository root. This offline reference needs no godoc server or published module.

AboutEnv type
AboutEnv is where the About page reads the process from. A zero field
is the real one; a test or a story sets them for a fixed page.

type AboutEnv struct {
	// BuildInfo is the build's module info (debug.ReadBuildInfo).
	BuildInfo func() (*debug.BuildInfo, bool)
	// Executable is the running binary's path (os.Executable).
	Executable func() (string, error)
	// PID is the process id (os.Getpid).
	PID int
	// Started is when the process started (when the package loaded).
	Started time.Time
	// Now is the clock the uptime reads (time.Now).
	Now func() time.Time
	// Open opens a path with the system's opener (open, xdg-open).
	Open func(path string) error
}
Source file
AboutPageID, AboutDepPrefixesSetting, AboutTabSetting, AboutPath constant
The About page: what this program is and where it runs. about.open opens
(or switches to) a framework page of sections of rows, each row a label
and a value: Build (the module, its version, commit, commit time, whether
the tree was modified, the Go version and the binary), then the sections
an application adds (AddAboutSection: its data directory, its daemon,
its extensions), Dependencies (every module of the build whose path
starts with a prefix in about.dep-prefixes, with where a replace or a
go.work points it) and Runtime (the pid, when it started, the uptime and
the status segments' last values). Rows are evaluated when the page
opens and on about.refresh, never at startup, so a row that asks a daemon
costs nothing until someone looks.

about.show answers the same sections as data (the CLI and MCP read it),
about.copy yanks them as text for a bug report. A row is an instance of
the about-row noun: about-row.copy yanks its value, about-row.open opens
a path row (Kind AboutPath) with the system opener, about-row.show
answers the row. All of them are read-only, so agents may run them.

With about.tab on (the default) the About page is a tab of the Settings
page (LinkSettingsTab): ] and [ step from the Settings page's last tab to
About and back, and the About page draws the Settings page's tab row.

const (
	// AboutPageID is the About page's instance id.
	AboutPageID = "__about"
	// AboutDepPrefixesSetting lists the module path prefixes whose
	// modules the Dependencies section shows.
	AboutDepPrefixesSetting = "about.dep-prefixes"
	// AboutTabSetting makes the About page a tab of the Settings page.
	AboutTabSetting = "about.tab"
	// AboutPath is the Kind of a row whose value is a file or directory:
	// about-row.open opens it.
	AboutPath = "path"
)

Action type
Action is one entry of actions.json: a verb on an existing noun that
runs an external command. It becomes an application operation
(noun.verb) like any other, so binding rows, the palette, the CLI, ?
help and the catalog see it; its work runs off the loop as a job
labelled Label, and the command's standard output is the job's result
(JSON when it parses, a JSON value per line when every line does, else
the text).

The command is either Command, an argv template run directly (never
through a shell), or Base, the convention for a CLI with a JSON form:
BASE NOUN VERB --json ARGS [--target TARGET], where NOUN VERB is Call
("noun verb", default this action's noun and verb), ARGS the action's
arguments as one JSON object and TARGET the Target template when it is
not empty. JSON appends --json ARGS to a Command too.

Templates ({{name}}) in Command, Base, Label, Dir and Target expand to:
target or id (the instance the verb runs on), label (its label, else
its id), noun and verb (the action's), data (the instance's Data: a string as is, else
JSON), data.KEY (one field of Data as a JSON object), args (the
arguments as JSON) or a declared parameter's value. An argv element that
is exactly one placeholder and expands to nothing is left out, so an
optional parameter disappears instead of passing "". Confirm is a
confirm prompt template, as NounBuilder.Confirm takes. History is the
kind of history entry it records (Operation.HistoryKind): "fetch" for a
command that only reads; empty is a change, as for any application verb.

Actions are read only from the user's own actions.json (Config.Path):
never from a catalog, a binding row, a socket call or an application's
data, so nothing but the user's file decides what runs.

An entry with an "alias" field is an alias row instead (AliasRow): one
more name for an existing action, which runs nothing of its own.

type Action struct {
	Noun        string               `json:"noun"`
	Verb        string               `json:"verb"`
	Description string               `json:"description,omitempty"`
	Command     Argv                 `json:"command,omitempty"`
	Base        Argv                 `json:"base,omitempty"`
	Call        string               `json:"call,omitempty"`
	Target      string               `json:"target,omitempty"`
	JSON        bool                 `json:"json,omitempty"`
	Label       string               `json:"label,omitempty"`
	Confirm     string               `json:"confirm,omitempty"`
	Parameters  map[string]Parameter `json:"parameters,omitempty"`
	Positional  []string             `json:"positional,omitempty"`
	Dir         string               `json:"dir,omitempty"`
	Timeout     string               `json:"timeout,omitempty"`
	Quiet       bool                 `json:"quiet,omitempty"`
	History     string               `json:"history,omitempty"`
	// Foreground gives the command the terminal (Foreground, foreground.go):
	// the TUI suspends while it runs (o-csv FILE, an editor) and its exit
	// status is told the user; no timeout, no captured output.
	Foreground bool `json:"foreground,omitempty"`
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}

ActionAlias type
ActionAlias is one extra name of a registered action. Alias is the
name (noun.verb, its noun canonical), Action the canonical key it runs.
Noun and Verb are the alias's own noun and verb: the subject side the
handler is told (Invocation). Since and RemoveAfter mark a deprecated
alias, an old name kept for one release after a rename (Keep actions):
its first use in a runtime posts a one-time notice naming the new name.
Note is free text shown beside it. Args are preset arguments the
alias runs its action with ({"force": true} for w!): a typed or
bound argument of the same name wins. Presets may leave a required
parameter out: it is asked for when the alias runs (the args form for a
key or the palette, NAME is required for the CLI and the API). Bare marks an alias declared
without a noun (w!, q): its noun is its action's, and the bare name
alone reaches it in the palette, the CLI and the API (Request{Verb:
"w!"}); a binding row names it by its full name (restructure.w!).
Shadows marks a bare alias that may take the name of a verb and yields
to it by what follows (set for settings.apply beside mode set): typed
alone, or followed by words its action's rest parameter reads, it is
the alias; followed by a noun that has the verb of its name, the line
is that noun's verb (set mode normal is mode set normal). Its name is
the bare word (set), since NOUN.VERB may be the verb it shadows.
Palette keeps a bare alias to the command line inside the app: the CLI
leaves the word to the shell's own commands, so bin help stays the
shell's help while :help opens the docs viewer, which needs the TUI.
ParamAliases are old names of its action's parameters the alias still
takes (ParamAlias): --starred for --value on star.

type ActionAlias struct {
	Alias        string       `json:"alias"`
	Action       string       `json:"action"`
	Noun         string       `json:"noun,omitempty"`
	Verb         string       `json:"verb,omitempty"`
	Since        string       `json:"since,omitempty"`
	RemoveAfter  string       `json:"removeAfter,omitempty"`
	Note         string       `json:"note,omitempty"`
	Source       string       `json:"source,omitempty"`
	Args         Arguments    `json:"args,omitempty"`
	Bare         bool         `json:"bare,omitempty"`
	Shadows      bool         `json:"shadows,omitempty"`
	Palette      bool         `json:"palette,omitempty"`
	ParamAliases []ParamAlias `json:"paramAliases,omitempty"`
}

Actor type
Actor identifies who issued a request. The zero value is the owner, preserving
requests and history written before attribution existed. Identity is advisory,
not authentication. Servers bind it once at startup, never from tool arguments.

AliasRow type
AliasRow is an alias entry of actions.json: {"alias": "pane.k",
"action": "pane.kill"} makes pane.k another name of pane.kill, for keys
(bindings.json rows), the palette, the CLI and the API. Alias is
noun.verb on a registered noun (a short noun name such as list works);
Action is a registered action or alias. An alias may not take the name
of an action or of another alias; such a row is skipped and reported, as
bindings check reports a bad row. Note is free text listed with it.
Shadows lets a bare alias take a verb's name and yield to it by what
follows (ActionAlias.Shadows); Palette keeps a bare alias out of the
CLI (ActionAlias.Palette).

type AliasRow struct {
	Alias   string    `json:"alias"`
	Action  string    `json:"action"`
	Note    string    `json:"note,omitempty"`
	Args    Arguments `json:"args,omitempty"`
	Shadows bool      `json:"shadows,omitempty"`
	Palette bool      `json:"palette,omitempty"`
	// ParamAliases are old names of the action's parameters the alias
	// takes (ActionAlias.ParamAliases): "paramAliases": [{"alias":
	// "starred", "param": "value"}].
	ParamAliases []ParamAlias `json:"paramAliases,omitempty"`
}
Source file

const (

	AliasShadowAllow  = "allow"

AliasShadowSetting constant
AliasShadowSetting says whether a bare alias may take the name of a
verb (a typed word): refuse (the default: such an alias row is skipped
and reported) or allow (the alias loads and, typed alone, wins over the
verb of that name; the verb stays reachable as noun verb).

const AliasShadowSetting = "aliases.shadow"

AppHandler type
AppHandler is the signature of an application verb: it reads the Ctx and
returns Done, Failed or Pending work. Pending work runs off the loop; its
completion is delivered to the owning instance through the contract, so a
handler never blocks key handling.

type AppHandler func(Ctx) Result

Appearance type
Appearance is event-loop-owned configuration. Operation results are value snapshots.
Dark is the terminal background reported by tea.BackgroundColorMsg; the
adaptive theme assumes a dark background until that message arrives.

Accent replaces the theme's accent colour when it is not nil: the title,
panel and modal borders, the active tab, the badge and every highlight
drawn in the accent take it, so an application can say "you are looking
at everything" by colour alone (a pin state). Semantic colours (errors)
and the muted text keep their meaning. The mono theme and NO_COLOR stay
colourless. Applications set it in code; the appearance verbs leave it.

type Appearance struct {
	Theme   string      `json:"theme"`
	Density string      `json:"density"`
	Dark    bool        `json:"dark"`
	Accent  color.Color `json:"-"`
	// Schemes are the colour schemes beyond the built-in themes, by name:
	// the user's themes.json (Model.Init, themes.reload and config.reload set it) or an
	// application's own (NewSchemeSet). A pointer keeps Appearance
	// comparable.
	Schemes *SchemeSet `json:"-"`
	// AppTones are the application's own tone rows (SetModeTone, SetTone),
	// laid over the built-in tones and under every themes.json entry, so
	// a theme still recolours them. A pointer keeps Appearance comparable.
	AppTones *ToneRows `json:"-"`
}
Source file
Appearance.Background function / method
Background is the background the appearance draws on, "light" or "dark"
(an adaptive scheme follows Dark), and whether it draws no colour at all
(the mono theme or NO_COLOR). A renderer with its own palette, such as
the markdown package's Glamour styles, picks from it.

func (a Appearance) Background() (background string, plain bool)
Source file
Appearance.SetModeTone function / method
SetModeTone declares the badge hue of a mode: the tone row
mode.<mode>, from which the rich and subtle chrome levels derive the
badge (rich fills it with hue, subtle draws hue on the surface; off
is unchanged). A themes.json theme's mode.<mode> row recolours it, and
its chrome.<level>.mode.<mode> row replaces a level's badge whole. A
mode with no hue row of its own takes the first row of its chain
(mode.pending for a pending mode, the mode it extends, mode).

func (a *Appearance) SetModeTone(mode string, hue SchemeColor)
Source file

SetTone declares (or replaces) the application's tone row name, laid

ArgumentError type
ArgumentError is a request refused for its arguments (a missing required
one, a wrong type, a value outside the enum or the source's choices)
before any instance was chosen, so a caller never blames the target for
it (the CLI adds no "pass --target" hint).

BareReading type
BareReading is how a command line that starts with a bare alias reads
(ReadBareAlias): the alias and its presets, with the presets of any
further bare aliases of the same action right after it (verbose set is
settings.apply {verbose: true}), Skip the words they took; or, for an
alias that shadows a verb, Noun: the line is that noun's verb of the
alias's name (set mode normal is mode set normal).

type BareReading struct {
	Alias ActionAlias
	Args  Arguments
	Skip  int
	Noun  string
}

Binding type
Binding is one row of a mode table, identical in code and config:
{noun | instance, mode, keys | pattern, request | null, from, when}.
Keys is an exact key or a space-separated sequence ("g g"); Pattern is a
key class: digit, alpha, printable, paste, any, a class minus keys
("alpha-q"), or a set ("one-of:h j k l"); upper is one uppercase
letter and ctrl+alpha one ctrl+letter (ctrl+a to ctrl+z). From fills request arguments from
the key or the mode payload (see Sources). When names a registered guard.
A row's identity is its table (noun or instance), mode, keys or pattern
and guard: a row replaces only the row with the same identity, so a row
with When and the unguarded row on the same keys coexist, and Unbind
removes only the row with the same identity (an unbind row without When
removes the unguarded row, never a guarded one). The editor noun's
implicit "input" guard is not part of the identity (see
withImplicitGuard).
Replay dispatches the key again, fresh, after the row ran (a pending mode
that hands an unknown key back to the stack). Capture rows are consulted
outer-first (app first) before the inner-first walk (see resolve.go).

A row is atomic: a Request (one registered operation, its arguments from
the row, the key or the payload), a mode transition (Enter, Leave) or an
unbind. There is no row that runs Go code with the key. Composite
behaviour is an operation whose handler calls other operations through
Execute (macro.record, terminal.paste under the leader); a condition is a
named guard. TestBindingRowsAreAtomic pins this for every table.

type Binding struct {
	// The field order is the bindings file's (BindingFieldOrder): mode,
	// the noun or instance, the keys or pattern, the request, then the
	// rest. FormatBindingRows writes a file in that order (and the
	// request noun before verb); plain json.Marshal follows the
	// declarations below.
	Mode     string            `json:"mode,omitempty"`
	Noun     string            `json:"noun,omitempty"`
	Instance string            `json:"instance,omitempty"`
	Keys     string            `json:"keys,omitempty"`
	Pattern  string            `json:"pattern,omitempty"`
	Request  Request           `json:"request"`
	When     string            `json:"when,omitempty"`
	Unbind   bool              `json:"unbind,omitempty"`
	From     map[string]string `json:"from,omitempty"`
	Replay   bool              `json:"replay,omitempty"`
	Capture  bool              `json:"capture,omitempty"`
	// Hint replaces "noun.verb: description" in the footer and the help
	// for a row whose operation says nothing useful on its own (the
	// terminal leader's mode.enter); bindings list carries it too.
	Hint string `json:"hint,omitempty"`
	// Hidden keeps the row off the footer's hint line (counts, registers,
	// macros, ctrl+c); ? help and bindings list still show it.
	Hidden bool `json:"hidden,omitempty"`
	// Help replaces the operation's description in ? help and bindings
	// list for a row whose operation is generic (mode.enter into a
	// register, macro or count mode).
	Help string `json:"help,omitempty"`
	// Aliases are the other names of the row's operation, which bindings
	// list adds so a row names its canonical operation and its aliases
	// once; a stored row never holds them (a file's value is dropped).
	Aliases []string `json:"aliases,omitempty"`
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}

Binding.FromRow function / method
FromRow fills an argument from the Data of the row the key acted on:
the innermost instance carrying Data from the focus out to the level the
row answered at (a list row's ListItem.Data, also when the row is bound
on the list). field names a key of that value (a map's key, a struct's
JSON field name; dots walk nested values, "repo.path"); "" passes the
whole value. A struct is read through its JSON encoding, so the value
is what the CLI would send (numbers float64, the whole row an object). A row without Data, or without the field, leaves the
argument out. The source is "row:<field>" (or "row") in a binding file.

func (b Binding) FromRow(arg, field string) Binding

BindingGroup type
BindingGroup is one table of a grouped bindings file: a noun's or an
instance's mode table and its rows in table order. Every row carries the
group's Noun or Instance and Mode.

type BindingGroup struct {
	Noun     string
	Instance string
	Mode     string
	Extends  string
	// Fallback ("fallback": true) keeps a default file's group to the keys
	// its table did not bind before the file loaded (LoadDefaultBindings):
	// an application's row bound earlier keeps its key.
	Fallback bool
	// Source ("source") is where the group's rows are declared, as
	// bindings defaults writes it; a reader keeps it and nothing acts on it.
	Source string
	Rows   []Binding
}

BindingHelp function / method
BindingHelp lists the rows a focus answers to in its current mode with
their descriptions, grouped by the noun of the level that answers, from
the innermost instance outward (the focused instance's rows, its noun's,
its parents' up to the page, then app; a capture row sits with its own
level, so app's ctrl+c comes after the focused row's verbs), and the
motions of every level last, under "movement". A row whose key enters a
pending mode (the terminal leader) is followed by that mode's rows as
whole sequences (helpEnters, R6.10).

func BindingHelp(r *Runtime, focus string) []HelpRow
Source file
BindingHints function / method
BindingHints renders the rows a focus answers to in its current mode as a
hint line, from the innermost level outward (the focused instance's rows,
its noun's, then its parents' up to the page and app):
"enter demo.open · : palette.open · ? bindings.help". Motions
(Operation.Motion) and hidden rows (counts, registers, macros, ctrl+c)
are left to ? help, except a motion row with its own Hint (J reads
"J/K detail"; a hint that starts with the row's key and a slash is shown
alone), and of rows that run the same request only the first
(innermost) key is shown.
It is the data bindings.list prints, so a hint never names a key that is
not bound; the descriptions are in BindingHelp (the ? modal).

func BindingHints(r *Runtime, focus string) string

Source file
Card type
Card is one entry of a timeline: a box with a header that always shows
(wrapped onto as many lines as it needs) and a meta line under it, and a
body shown only while the card is open. Like a ListItem, a card is an
instance of its noun (Noun, default TimelineCardNoun) under the
timeline's list, so the app's verbs and rows on that noun answer on it
and Data is what a verb reads as Ctx.Instance.Data. Label is the
instance's label (palette, {{label}}, the / filter), default the header
on one line.

Body and every section's Body are asked lazily, with the inner width,
only while the card is open and drawn (or scrolled across); the result
is kept until the width, the folds or Revision change, so an app bumps
Revision whenever a body's text changes (a streaming answer). Open is the
card's state the first time the timeline sees its id; after that the
user's open and close follow the id. Active draws the card brighter (its
header bold, a "●" in its top border, which reads in mono too); Muted
draws its header and meta in the muted style (an older card). A card
whose Group differs from the card above it is preceded by a full-width
rule with the group as its label (a session boundary).

Steps are the places inside an open card that l steps onto and j/k walk
(timeline_steps.go). A card with Steps draws its body from them, and
Body and Sections are not drawn; a card without them steps through its
Body's paragraphs (blank-line separated, a fenced code block one
paragraph) and then each section, title and text, as one step.

type Card struct {
	ID       string                 `json:"id"`
	Noun     string                 `json:"noun,omitempty"`
	Label    string                 `json:"label,omitempty"`
	Data     any                    `json:"data,omitempty"`
	Header   string                 `json:"header"`
	Meta     string                 `json:"meta,omitempty"`
	Body     func(width int) string `json:"-"`
	Sections []CardSection          `json:"sections,omitempty"`
	Steps    []CardStep             `json:"steps,omitempty"`
	Open     bool                   `json:"open,omitempty"`
	Active   bool                   `json:"active,omitempty"`
	Muted    bool                   `json:"muted,omitempty"`
	Group    string                 `json:"group,omitempty"`
	Revision uint64                 `json:"revision,omitempty"`
	// Event draws the card as something that happened rather than
	// something said: indented one level (eventIndent cells), its border
	// dashed and muted until it is selected. A task's notification is one.
	Event bool `json:"event,omitempty"`
	// MetaTones tone runs of Meta (a status word, its glyph) as a list
	// row's ToneSpan tones a cell: From and To are rune offsets into Meta
	// (To 0: its end), Cell is not read, a later span wins where two
	// overlap. A toned run draws in its tone's style instead of the muted
	// meta style; the text never changes, so widths and copies are the
	// same as without tones. An unknown tone draws muted, as the rest.
	MetaTones []ToneSpan `json:"metaTones,omitempty"`
	// Tone is a tone whose style draws the card's border (and the Active
	// "●" in it) in place of the border role: a card whose state should
	// be seen from across the timeline (a running turn, a failed one).
	// The chrome row border.<tone> of the chrome.color level draws it when
	// the level has one (chrome.rich.border.state.working), else the tone.
	// The focused timeline's selected card keeps the accent, so the
	// focus always reads; an unknown tone, or "", draws the usual border.
	Tone string `json:"tone,omitempty"`
}

CardSection type
CardSection is one foldable part of an open card's body, drawn after
Body: a title line ("▾ 2 thinking · Summary") and, unfolded, its text
indented in its Role's style. Name keys its fold state (default the
title, else its number); Folded is its state until the user folds it.

type CardSection struct {
	Name    string                 `json:"name,omitempty"`
	Title   string                 `json:"title"`
	Summary string                 `json:"summary,omitempty"`
	Role    CardRole               `json:"role,omitempty"`
	Folded  bool                   `json:"folded,omitempty"`
	Body    func(width int) string `json:"-"`
}
Source file
CardStep type
CardStep is one place inside an open card (Card.Steps), in order.

  - Header: the step is the card's header (a prompt): it draws nothing
    of its own, its lines are the header's and its text is Header.
  - A Title draws a bold title line; with a Body the step folds like a
    section ("▾ 2 tool Bash · summary", z 2, timeline.fold {section}
    by ID, title or number), Folded its first state, and its body is
    indented under the title. Folded, the title is the whole step; l
    inside opens it.
  - Paragraphs splits the Body into steps of its own, one per paragraph
    (blank-line separated, a fenced code block one paragraph); under a
    Title the title line belongs to the first.
  - Without a Title the Body draws at the full width in Role.

Steps follow each other with a blank line between them, except two
titled steps in a row (they stack as sections do). ID keys the fold.

type CardStep struct {
	ID         string                 `json:"id,omitempty"`
	Title      string                 `json:"title,omitempty"`
	Summary    string                 `json:"summary,omitempty"`
	Role       CardRole               `json:"role,omitempty"`
	Folded     bool                   `json:"folded,omitempty"`
	Header     bool                   `json:"header,omitempty"`
	Paragraphs bool                   `json:"paragraphs,omitempty"`
	Body       func(width int) string `json:"-"`
}

ChooserBatch type
ChooserBatch is a streaming load's value: a source's Load (or the
Pending work it returns) gives one part of its rows, shown at once, and
More, pending work whose value is the next ChooserBatch (or the last
[]ChooserRow). The chooser appends each part as it lands and stays
loading until a part has no More; a newer load or closing the chooser
cancels the rest. A walk over a folder tree streams this way, so the
first rows show before it finishes and the list stays responsive.

type ChooserBatch struct {
	Rows []ChooserRow
	More Result
}

ChooserRememberSourceSetting, ChooserEnterSetting constant
Chooser settings: rows of settings/ochazuke.json.

const (
	// ChooserRememberSourceSetting reopens a chooser with sources on the
	// source last used for its kind (on), or on its first source (off).
	ChooserRememberSourceSetting = "chooser.remember-source"
	// ChooserEnterSetting is what enter does on a row that can be both
	// opened and picked (a folder in a folder chooser): "open" (the
	// default; alt+enter picks) or "pick" (ctrl+l opens).
	ChooserEnterSetting = "chooser.enter"
)
Source file
ChooserRow type
ChooserRow is one choice. Trailing rows (a "browse…" entry) follow the
matches. Enter takes one without moving onto it only when the query is
not empty and no ordinary row matches, so their filter should be strict
(a substring of the label); an empty query with no ordinary rows creates.
Fixed rows (a "no pin" entry) sit at the bottom whatever the query: the
filter never sees them and Enter takes one only once the highlight is
moved onto it, or when the query spells it.

type ChooserRow struct {
	// Noun names the row's declared props and saved filters; empty uses ChooserSpec.Noun.
	Noun string
	// Props supplies typed values when Data is an opaque handle, as on ListItem.
	Props    Thing
	ID       string
	Label    string
	Data     any
	Trailing bool
	Fixed    bool
	// Cells, when set, draw the row as aligned columns (each column as
	// wide as its widest cell among the rows); Label stays what the
	// filter reads.
	Cells []string
	// Opens marks a row its source can open (ChooserSource.Open: a
	// folder to browse into): chooser.open opens it, and so does enter
	// unless the setting chooser.enter is "pick". NoPick marks a row that
	// only opens (a folder in a file chooser): picking it opens it.
	Opens  bool
	NoPick bool
	// Refuse marks a row that cannot be picked and says why: picking it
	// fails with Refuse and the chooser stays open (a typed path whose
	// folder is missing, R7.8).
	Refuse string
	// Tones colour runs of the row through the theme's tones, as a list
	// row's ToneSpan does (tone.go): Cell indexes Cells, or is 0 for the
	// Label when the row has no Cells; From and To are rune offsets into
	// that text (an app dims a path prefix, "parent › "). The highlighted
	// row keeps them over its reverse bar, as a list's selected row does.
	Tones []ToneSpan
}
Source file
ChooserSource type
ChooserSource is one source of a chooser's rows.

type ChooserSource struct {
	// Name is what the title calls the source ("browse", "recent").
	Name string
	// Label, when set, is what the title shows for the current source in
	// place of Name ("browse ~/proj").
	Label func() string
	// Load lists the source's rows for the query: Done([]ChooserRow),
	// Failed (the error is the empty line), or Pending work whose value is
	// a []ChooserRow, run off the loop as a job of the chooser (a newer
	// load replaces it; closing the chooser cancels it).
	Load func(query string) Result
	// Live sources match the query themselves: Load runs again on every
	// edit and its rows are shown as they come, in their order. Other
	// sources load once when they become current (and after Open or Up)
	// and the chooser's Filter narrows their rows.
	Live bool
	// Empty is the line shown while the source has no rows ("no recent
	// paths"); the spec's Empty (or "loading…" while a load runs) else.
	Empty string
	// Open navigates into a row marked Opens (browse into a folder) and
	// returns the query to show next; false leaves everything as it is.
	// The source then loads again.
	Open func(row ChooserRow) (query string, ok bool)
	// Up navigates back out (the parent folder) given the query and
	// returns the query to show next; false when there is nowhere to go.
	Up func(query string) (string, bool)
	// Create and CreateLabel, when set, stand in for the spec's while the
	// source is current (a browse source creates in its folder).
	Create      func(query string) Result
	CreateLabel func(query string) string
	// Keep, when set, stands in for the spec's Keep while the source is
	// current: the cell columns cut last (a known-folder source's path,
	// its branch and project columns narrowing first).
	Keep []int
}
Source file
ChooserSourcesGuard, ChooserOpensGuard, ChooserEnterOpensGuard, ChooserUpGuard, ChooserUpEmptyGuard constant
Chooser guards: the rows of chooser.json name them.

const (
	ChooserSourcesGuard    = "chooser-sources"     // more than one source
	ChooserOpensGuard      = "chooser-opens"       // the highlighted row opens
	ChooserEnterOpensGuard = "chooser-enter-opens" // enter opens it (ChooserEnterSetting, or a row that only opens)
	ChooserUpGuard         = "chooser-up"          // the source can step up
	ChooserUpEmptyGuard    = "chooser-up-empty"    // it can, and the query is empty (backspace)
)
Source file
ChooserSpec type
ChooserSpec opens a chooser. Kind is the modal's kind (one open chooser
per kind). Title is the modal's title. Filter defaults to a fuzzy match
on Label, case as the search.case setting says (CaseFor; a Filter of the
application's own can read Runtime.CaseFor too); matches whose label
holds the query whole come first, otherwise in Rows order. Create is optional; without it Enter on no match fails.
CreateLabel names what Enter would create from the query. CreateRow shows
that as a "+ <label>" row while the query is not empty, after the matches
and trailing rows and before the fixed rows; typing never matches or
ranks it, it is the default only when nothing else matches, and Enter on
it creates. Empty is the line shown while there are no rows at all
("loading…"). Keep names the cell columns cut last. Preview draws a
preview of the highlighted row beside the rows when there is room.

type ChooserSpec struct {
	// Noun is the default row noun for prop filtering.
	Noun string
	// NoPropFilters opts this chooser out of prop filters and their keys.
	NoPropFilters bool

	Kind        string
	Title       string
	Rows        []ChooserRow
	Filter      func(query string, row ChooserRow) bool
	Choose      func(ChooserRow) Result
	Create      func(query string) Result
	CreateLabel func(query string) string
	CreateRow   bool
	Empty       string
	// Keep names the columns (indexes into ChooserRow.Cells) cut last when
	// the cells do not fit (a directory chooser's path): the others narrow
	// to their minimum first, then the kept ones, widest first.
	Keep []int
	// Preview, when set, draws the highlighted row's text beside the rows
	// (wrapped, cut at the bottom) once the chooser is ChooserPreviewMin
	// cells wide; narrower, the rows take the whole width.
	Preview func(ChooserRow) string
	// Sources, when set, supply the rows one source at a time (see
	// ChooserSource; Rows is then the current source's). Source names
	// the one to open on; else (or when this open has no source of that
	// name) the one last used for the kind (chooser.remember-source),
	// else the first.
	Sources []ChooserSource
	Source  string
}
Source file
ChooserStableHeightSetting constant
ChooserStableHeightSetting keeps an open chooser at the height it
reached (settings/ochazuke.json).

const ChooserStableHeightSetting = "chooser.stable-height"
Source file
ChromeColorSetting, ChromeOff, ChromeSubtle, ChromeRich constant
Chrome colour: how much colour the chrome (borders, the mode badge, the
hint lines) draws is the setting chrome.color, a level: off, subtle or
rich. A level is a family of tone rows, chrome.<level>.<role> (the role
border.focused at rich is the tone chrome.rich.border.focused), so every
colour is data: the built-in rows are in chromeTones, and a themes.json
entry overrides any of them as it does any tone. A role with no row at
the level draws as it did before the levels existed, so off, which has
no rows, is that look exactly.

A chrome row's colours may name the palette roles a tone names, plus
mode: the colour of the current mode's badge (its row's background,
else its foreground). An application's accent (Appearance.Accent)
replaces the accent, border and mode roles in chrome rows, as it
replaces the accent everywhere else.

Roles (the fallback is the look before the levels):

	border                      an unfocused panel's border
	border.focused              a focused panel's border (heavy lines stay)
	border.<tone>               a panel given a tone (Layout.WithPanelTone), a card's (Card.Tone)
	border.modal                a modal's border
	border.completion           the completion popup's border
	border.split                the path picker's split rule
	panel.title                 an unfocused panel's title
	panel.title.focused         a focused panel's title
	title.mark, title           the title row's ◆ and the title
	mode.<name>, mode.pending,  the mode badge: the first row that exists of
	mode.<extends>, mode        the mode's own, pending (a pending mode),
	                            the mode it extends, then mode
	hint.key, hint.text,        the footer's and every modal's hint line:
	hint.sep                    a row's key, its action, the " · " between

const (
	// ChromeColorSetting is the chrome colour level, a row of
	// settings/ochazuke.json (appearance).
	ChromeColorSetting = "chrome.color"
	ChromeOff          = "off"
	ChromeSubtle       = "subtle"
	ChromeRich         = "rich"
)
Source file

ChromeLevels are chrome.color's values.

var ChromeLevels = []string{ChromeOff, ChromeSubtle, ChromeRich}

Source file
Column type
Column is one column of a table. Width fixes the column's cells (0: a
flexible column, as wide as its widest cell or title when there is
room); Min is a flexible column's least width before columns drop
(default: 10 cells or its title's width when wider, at most its widest
cell or title). Priority orders
dropping when the width is short: the column with the largest Priority
drops first, ties the rightmost first; 0, the default, is the most
important, and the last column left never drops (it is cut instead).
Max caps a flexible column's growth (0: up to its widest cell). Wrap
makes List.View wrap a cell longer than its column onto further lines
of the row instead of cutting it: the row is as tall as its tallest
wrapped cell, the other cells sit on its first line (TableText still
cuts); a newline in its cell is a line break. Lines makes List.View
break a cell at its newlines and cut each line at the column's width
("…"), so a cell of one item per line keeps an item per line (the
help's arguments); with Wrap too, a long line wraps instead. Group makes List.View draw a cell blank when it repeats the cell
above it in the same section (and every Group column left of it does
too), so a run of rows sharing a value (the help's key prefix) shows it
once; the first row a frame draws always shows its cells, and the rows'
Cells keep the value (TableText does not blank). Part marks a column
that is not data (PartRowNumber, a row-number column; any word an
application picks): its cells draw in the tone "list.<part>" and, where
a highlight crosses them, "list.<part>.intersect" (ToneSpan.Layer;
setting list.part-tones, default on).

type Column struct {
	Title    string `json:"title"`
	Width    int    `json:"width,omitempty"`
	Min      int    `json:"min,omitempty"`
	Max      int    `json:"max,omitempty"`
	Align    Align  `json:"align,omitempty"`
	Priority int    `json:"priority,omitempty"`
	Wrap     bool   `json:"wrap,omitempty"`
	Lines    bool   `json:"lines,omitempty"`
	Pinned   bool   `json:"pinned,omitempty"`
	Group    bool   `json:"group,omitempty"`
	Part     string `json:"part,omitempty"`
}
Source file

const ColumnWidthStep = 2

}
Source file
CompletionSearch type
CompletionSearch answers a query, the token after its trigger, with
suggestions. It runs off the loop, as a job; its context is cancelled
when the answer can no longer be shown (the suggestions were dismissed,
or a newer answer is already on screen). An error is shown in the list.

type CompletionSearch func(ctx context.Context, query string) ([]Suggestion, error)
Source file
CompletionToken type
CompletionToken is the trigger token under an input's cursor: the rune
range [Start, End) it covers, its trigger and its query (the token after
the trigger).

type CompletionToken struct {
	Start   int    `json:"start"`
	End     int    `json:"end"`
	Trigger string `json:"trigger"`
	Query   string `json:"query"`
}
Source file
CompletionTrigger type
CompletionTrigger is one trigger of an input: the characters that start
a completion token and the search that answers its query.

type CompletionTrigger struct {
	Trigger string
	Search  CompletionSearch
	// SearchAt, when set in place of Search, answers with the whole text
	// and cursor as well as the query (CompletionQuery).
	SearchAt func(ctx context.Context, q CompletionQuery) ([]Suggestion, error)
	// DropTrigger makes accepting replace the trigger too: the
	// suggestion's Text is all that stays (a "\" that only asks for a
	// symbol). Off, the default, keeps it.
	DropTrigger bool
	// Boundaries are the characters besides whitespace that end a token
	// (both ways), so the trigger can follow one directly: "|(,"
	// lets a jq "." start after a pipe or an open paren. "" is
	// whitespace only. They may not hold the trigger's characters.
	Boundaries string
	// Every makes the trigger complete every token, its Trigger empty: a
	// field whose words all come from a list (a filter form's values).
	// An empty Trigger without Every is refused.
	Every bool
}
Source file

Component is the delivery contract every instance that works off the loop
implements through Instance.Component. HandleMessage applies a result on
the loop and reports whether the message was for this component;
PendingCommand hands the Bubble Tea adapter the work scheduled since the
last drain; Close cancels outstanding work and releases anything held.
The picker and the registers component are the framework's own; an
application adds its own by setting Instance.Component.

type Component interface {
	HandleMessage(tea.Msg) bool

	Close()

Config type
Config is the application's per-user configuration directory and what
the framework loaded from it: the user bindings file (bindings.json,
rows in the bindings.load shape that replace or unbind the defaults by
noun, mode and keys) and the declared settings (settings.json). It is
one instance under app, reached through Runtime.Config(), never a
Runtime field.

The directory is Dir when set, else $XDG_CONFIG_HOME/<App>, else
~/.config/<App>. App defaults to the binary's name (a test binary,
NAME.test, has none), so every application gets the files without code;
an application whose directory is named otherwise sets App before the
Model starts (mtm sets "matomeru"). With App and Dir both empty there is
no directory: nothing is read or written.

type Config struct {
	App string
	Dir string
	// Report, when set, receives every problem as it is found: a bindings
	// row that was skipped, a file that does not parse, an unknown or
	// invalid setting. NewModel sets it to Model.Notify, so problems are
	// persistent footer notices.
	Report func(problem string)
	// Withdraw, when set, takes back a problem Report sent once it no
	// longer holds (bindings.reload of a fixed file); NewModel sets it to
	// drop the notice.
	Withdraw func(problem string)
	// StateDir, when set, is the state directory (the history file);
	// else $XDG_STATE_HOME/<App>, else ~/.local/state/<App>.
	StateDir string
	// NoHistoryFile keeps the action history in memory only: an
	// application opts out of history.jsonl.
	NoHistoryFile bool
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}

Ctx type
Ctx is what an application handler receives: the runtime, the context the
request runs in, its arguments and the instance it addresses (id, noun,
payload).

type Ctx struct {
	Runtime  *Runtime
	Context  Context
	Args     Arguments
	Instance *Instance
	// Subjects are the instances the request acts on: Instance alone,
	// or, for a verb that takes many (Operation.Many) run over a visual
	// selection, every selected row in display order (Instance among them).
	Subjects []*Instance
	// Invoked is the name the request ran the verb by: its own key, or an
	// alias (an old name, a user's actions.json alias, the other noun of
	// an operation reachable under two nouns), with that name's noun as
	// the subject side.
	Invoked Invocation
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}

Ctx.Each function / method
Each is how a verb that takes many subjects (Operation.Many) makes its
change once it knows what to do (an argument given, a chooser
answered): one runs for every subject in order, each with a Ctx narrowed
to that subject (Instance, Context and Subjects), so the one-subject
body stays as it was. A run over several posts the notice a per-row run
would (subjectsNotice): one info, or one error counting the done and the
failed. A body must answer synchronously (Done or Failed); pending work
counts as a failure. On one subject Each is that subject's Result.

func (ctx Ctx) Each(one AppHandler) Result

Detail type
Detail is the component a detail instance fronts: a page's read-only
detail region (the text beside or below a list about its selected row)
that scrolls while the focus stays in the list. The application names
the item the detail is about with the func it gives AddDetail (the
selected row's id) and draws the text through Window; the framework
keeps the offset. A new item starts at the top, checked on every Window
and every scroll, so a scroll typed before the next frame lands on the
new item; the same item keeps its offset while its text refreshes
(clamped to the new length). J and K on
the app noun (detail.scroll, counts work: 10J) scroll the current page's
detail while it overflows and the focus is on the page outside it; a
focused noun that binds J or K (the outline) keeps them, as inner rows
answer first. A detail is not a focus stop.

type Detail struct {
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}

Source file
Digit, Alpha, Printable, Upper, CtrlAlpha, Paste, Any constant
Pattern names.

const (
	Digit     = "digit"
	Alpha     = "alpha"
	Printable = "printable"
	// Upper is one uppercase letter (A to Z, any script's capital): it
	// answers before Alpha, which an uppercase letter also belongs to.
	Upper = "upper"
	// CtrlAlpha is one ctrl+letter, ctrl+a to ctrl+z: one row for every
	// control letter, the letter read with the letter source. A legacy
	// terminal sends ctrl+h, ctrl+i, ctrl+j and ctrl+m as backspace, tab
	// and enter, and ctrl+[ as esc, so those never reach the row there;
	// labels a row reads this way should avoid h, i, j and m.

FitFocusPath function / method
FitFocusPath joins levels with " › " in at most width cells. When they
do not fit, it cuts from the left a whole level at a time behind "… › ",
keeping the innermost level; with less room the innermost level alone
follows "…", cut from its left when it is wider still. A cut path always
starts with "…". Width under 1 is "".

(Submit) whose every field is valid (form-runnable), form.next-missing
on one with a field still not valid (form-missing). On such a form one
of the two always is, so enter's rows (run, next-missing, the field's

Guard type
Guard is a named condition: an action's availability (Available, the
verb guard) or, the older use kept while areas move, a condition a row
requires (Binding.When; bindings check lists such rows as debts). The built-in guards are
"input" and "no-input" (the focus is or is not an input), "multiline", "count" and "no-count" (the
pending count is present or absent), "self" (the focus is the instance
whose level the row sits on, not a descendant), "shadow-idle" (the mode a
shadow mode such as macro_recording re-dispatches in is normal with an
empty payload) and "op:<key>" (the pending operator). Applications add
their own with DefineGuard.

type Guard func(*Runtime, KeyEvent) bool

Header type
Header is the top of the page chrome: the title row with an optional
right-aligned badge (the shell shows the mode there) and the subtitle row,
which is a label or, when Tabs is set, the page tabs with Active marked.
The badge is always the right end of the title row. Focus is the focus
left of it (the focus-display setting): its levels, outermost first,
drawn dim and cut from the left (FitFocusPath) so the innermost level
stays. Segments are the status
segments (Runtime.SegmentCells), drawn right-aligned in the rule under
the header (HeaderRule); they take no row. Every field is a raw label.

type Header struct {
	Title    string
	Badge    string
	Focus    []string
	Subtitle string
	Tabs     []string
	Active   int
	Segments []SegmentCell
	// Keys is the key echo when keys.echo puts it beside the badge.
	Keys *KeyEchoCell
	// BadgeWidth is the fewest cells the badge's label takes: a shorter
	// label is padded inside the badge, so the badge keeps its left edge
	// as the mode changes (the shell passes Runtime.ModeBadgeWidth).
	BadgeWidth int
}

Source file
HelpHere, HelpPick, HelpTable constant
The ? help's views. Here is what the focus answers to now (BindingHelp,
the default). Pick is the same table for a chosen noun and mode, as if
the focus were on that noun in that mode (NounHelp). Table is a chosen
noun's keys by mode: a row per key or key class, a column per mode with
rows for the noun or app, each cell the operation that answers there.
S and tab (bindings-help.view {step}; shift+tab backwards) cycle the
views the settings leave on; n (bindings-help.noun) chooses the noun of
pick and table, m (bindings-help.mode) the mode of pick, p
(bindings-help.place) the place of pick and table: one of the noun's
recorded places (NounPlaces), whose chain gives the walk the levels
between the noun and app, or no place.
All four are rows on the help modal's noun, guarded so they answer, and
are hinted, only where they do something.

const (
	HelpHere  = "here"
	HelpPick  = "pick"
	HelpTable = "table"
)
Source file

HelpInsertKey opens the bindings help in insert mode, where ? types
itself: an app row NewModel adds beside ? in normal mode. ctrl+\ is bound
by no Readline default, no editor, palette, chooser, completion, filter,
form or outline row, and the terminal is in raw mode, so it sends no
SIGQUIT.

const HelpInsertKey = "ctrl+\\"

Source file
HelpRow type
HelpRow is one binding as the help shows it: the key, the operation it
runs (noun.verb, or the row's Hint when it has one), arguments resolved
when help opens, its description, the answering noun, and the help's
section: that noun, or "movement" for a motion (Operation.Motion).
Prefix and Final are Key split by SplitKeys (the help's prefix and key
columns); Request is the operation the row runs (noun.verb, the noun
by its shortest name, also when Operation shows the Hint; "" for a mode
transition) and Hint the row's own Hint.

type HelpRow struct {
	Key         string    `json:"key"`
	Prefix      string    `json:"prefix,omitempty"`
	Final       string    `json:"final,omitempty"`
	Operation   string    `json:"operation"`
	Request     string    `json:"request,omitempty"`
	Hint        string    `json:"hint,omitempty"`
	Args        Arguments `json:"args,omitempty"`
	Description string    `json:"description,omitempty"`
	Noun        string    `json:"noun"`
	Section     string    `json:"section,omitempty"`
	// Guard is the guard a hypothetical row (the help's pick and table
	// views) answers under; the live help judges guards and leaves it out.
	Guard string `json:"when,omitempty"`
	// Aliases are the other names of the row's operation (ActionAlias),
	// listed once on the row and after its description ("also: …"); the
	// row itself always names the canonical operation.
	Aliases []string `json:"aliases,omitempty"`
	// Enters is the pending mode the row's key enters when the help lists
	// that mode's rows after it (the terminal leader: leader_pending).
	Enters string `json:"enters,omitempty"`
	// Group marks a prefix row: its key starts longer sequences (g of
	// g c, the leader ctrl+a), which are listed Under it. A prefix bound
	// to nothing alone is a row helpGroups makes, with no Operation.
	Group bool `json:"group,omitempty"`
	// Under is the prefix row this row is listed under (its longest
	// prefix that is a Group row: "ctrl+a" for "ctrl+a v", "g c" for
	// "g c x"); "" at the top of its section. Key is the whole sequence.
	Under string `json:"under,omitempty"`
	// Steps are a sequence's keys one by one, each with what it does at
	// that point: entering a mode, waiting for the next key, then the
	// final operation. A single key has none.
	Steps []HelpStep `json:"steps,omitempty"`
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}
Source file

HelpStep is one key of a sequence as the help's steps show it: the key,
and what it does at that point in the sequence: the pending mode it

type HelpStep struct {
	Key         string `json:"key"`
	Operation   string `json:"operation,omitempty"`
	Mode        string `json:"mode,omitempty"`
	Description string `json:"description"`

HistoryAction, HistoryChange, HistoryFetch, HistorySystem constant
The kinds of history entry (HistoryEntry.Kind, Operation.HistoryKind).
An action is the user's interaction (a motion, a focus, page or modal
move, a mode, the palette's line), a change is an edit or a mutating verb
(what dot-repeat reads), a fetch asks for data (a state, list or show
verb, a reload, a host calling an extension). HistorySystem is reserved
for what the runtime does on its own (a resize, a job that finished); no
entry has it yet.

const (
	HistoryAction = "action"
	HistoryChange = "change"
	HistoryFetch  = "fetch"
	HistorySystem = "system"
)

HistoryContext is the interaction state a request ran in. Source is

type HistoryContext struct {
	Actor  Actor  `json:"actor,omitzero"`
	Focus  string `json:"focus"`
	Page   string `json:"page,omitempty"`
	Mode   string `json:"mode"`
	Source string `json:"source"`
}
Source file
HistoryEntry type
HistoryEntry is one user action: what ran, where, from which source, how
it ended and, when it moved the interaction, from where to where (Moved).
It holds ids and the request, never the data the operation returned, so
an entry costs the same over a list of ten rows or ten thousand. Kind is
the operation's HistoryKind. Job is the job id an application verb
launched.

type HistoryEntry struct {
	Seq     uint64         `json:"seq"`
	At      time.Time      `json:"at"`
	Kind    string         `json:"kind,omitempty"`
	Request Request        `json:"request"`
	Context HistoryContext `json:"context"`
	Result  HistoryResult  `json:"result"`
	Moved   *HistoryMove   `json:"moved,omitempty"`
	Job     uint64         `json:"job,omitempty"`
	// Depth is how many requests enclose this one: a request an operation
	// runs while it runs (a choices source, a macro's keys) is one deeper.
	Depth int `json:"depth,omitempty"`
	// Restored marks an entry read from the history file at start
	// (EnsureHistory): an earlier session ran it. Dot-repeat skips it.
	Restored bool `json:"restored,omitempty"`
	// Changes are what a standard write did to each thing, before and
	// after (standard_undo.go): what history.undo reverts.
	Changes []ThingChange `json:"changes,omitempty"`
}
Source file

the ring while it is persisted): the last N entries are kept; 0 writes
and reads no file.

const HistorySetting = "history-limit"

HookChainLimit constant
HookChainLimit bounds the events one burst of hooks may cause (a hook
whose verb closes a modal emits surface.left, which may run another
hook): past it the rest are dropped with a notice, so two rows that feed

Source file
HookRow type
HookRow attaches a verb to an event: when On is emitted and every Where
field matches, Do runs with Args, plus each From argument read from the
event's field it names (HookAllFields: the whole event).

A where value matches a field that equals it (strings, numbers and
booleans compared as text) or, for a list field such as changed, that
holds it; a where value that is a list matches any of its values. That is
all the logic there is: no expressions.

Source is who declared the row: "user" for hooks.json, the application
("app") or the extension that declared a default. It is ignored in the
file, so an exported file loads back as is.

type HookRow struct {
	On     string            `json:"on"`
	Where  map[string]any    `json:"where,omitempty"`
	Do     string            `json:"do,omitempty"`
	Args   Arguments         `json:"args,omitempty"`
	From   map[string]string `json:"from,omitempty"`
	Off    bool              `json:"off,omitempty"`
	Source string            `json:"source,omitempty"`
}
Source file

Matches reports whether every where field matches fields.

func (h HookRow) Matches(fields map[string]any) bool

Source file
ImplicitGuard constant
ImplicitGuard is the guard the framework puts on every editor noun row
that runs an input operation (input.cancel aside): such a row answers
only while the focus is an input itself, not a non-input instance an
application parented under one. It is the framework's, not the user's:
it is not part of a row's identity, so a row without when replaces or
unbinds the default it shadows, and a user row that runs an input
operation on the editor noun gets it too, as the default it replaces had.

const ImplicitGuard = "input"

Input type
Input is a single- or multi-line modal text field with an undo tree.

type Input struct {
	ID         string
	Text       []rune
	Cursor     int
	Mode       string // the editing mode: normal, insert or a visual mode; pending modes live on the instance
	Multiline  bool
	Kind       string
	PathFilter string
	// Secret marks an input whose text is never shown outside it: the key
	// echo (keys.echo) shows nothing at all while it has the focus, not
	// even how much was typed. A record editor field over a Secret prop
	// sets it (RecordField.Secret).
	Secret bool
	// Completions are the input's completion triggers (Completions, the
	// AddInput option); none falls back to its noun's.
	Completions []CompletionTrigger
	// ReadOnly makes the input a viewer: the cursor, motions, visual
	// selections and yanks work, every edit is a silent no-op (Replace
	// leaves the text, insert mode is not entered, a delete or change
	// operator neither deletes nor yanks), and the editing verbs are
	// unavailable at it (guard input-writable), so their rows stand aside
	// and leave the hints. Like Vim's 'nomodifiable', without E21: nothing
	// is reported. Set it before or after AddInput (or the ReadOnly option).
	ReadOnly bool
	Anchor   int
	History  []EditNode
	Head     int
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}

Source file
Input.SelectText function / method
SelectText opens a select range over the runes [start, end) in insert
mode, as Vim's select mode does over a new name: the first text typed
(input.insert, a paste) replaces the range and typing continues in
insert; backspace or delete removes it; a motion, a mode change (esc),
an undo or any other edit closes it and keeps the text. The range is
drawn in the visual-selection style. It is a state of the input, not a
mode: the person is inserting, and the mode stays insert. An empty range
selects nothing and only enters insert at end.

func (in *Input) SelectText(start, end int) error
Source file
Input.SelectedText function / method
SelectedText is the open select range (SelectText), if any.

func (in *Input) SelectedText() (start, end int, ok bool)
Source file
Input.Selection function / method
Selection is the visual selection's rune range [start, end) while the
input is in a character or line visual mode (v, V): the runes under
the anchor and the cursor are both in it, a line selection covers whole
lines and its last newline. ok is false outside a visual mode and in a
block selection (visual-block), which is not one range (its snapshot names the
rows and columns). Hosts read it instead of recomputing it from Anchor
and Cursor.

func (in *Input) Selection() (start, end int, ok bool)
Source file
Input.SetMode function / method
SetMode is the mode-transition hook for both editor operations and set.mode.
Direct assignment to Mode cannot delimit insert sessions.

func (in *Input) SetMode(mode string)
Source file
Input.SetText function / method
SetText puts text in the input from the program, read-only or not: a
viewer refreshed with what it shows (a jq panel following the query).
The text becomes the undo tree's root (nothing to undo back to); the
cursor and a visual selection's anchor keep their offsets, clamped into
the new text as a motion would leave them (in normal mode not past the
line's last character); an open select range and an insert session end.
A single-line input keeps the text up to its first line break. The same
text again changes nothing, so the undo history stays.

func (in *Input) SetText(text string)

Source file
Instance type
Instance is one addressable thing on screen: a page, a list, a list row, a
modal body, or an input. Parent links form the focus stack; walking them
from the focused instance outward is the path Context.Path spells out.
An instance holds no mode: the mode is global (Runtime.Mode). Payload is
the data a modal body carries (a confirm's prompt and request). Bindings
are this instance's own rows, resolved above its noun's table at the same
stack level. Component holds the typed component the instance fronts.
Unfocusable opts the instance out of being a focus stop although its noun
is Focusable (a disabled panel). Label is an optional display name a
confirm prompt reads as {{label}}. Data is an opaque application value
(a list row's ListItem.Data); the framework never reads it. Pin is the
base mode the instance declares (Runtime.PinInstance): focus landing on
it, or beneath it with no nearer pin, puts the runtime in that mode, and
mode.set {mode: declared} returns to it.

type Instance struct {
	ID          string    `json:"id"`
	Noun        string    `json:"noun"`
	Parent      string    `json:"parent,omitempty"`
	Payload     Payload   `json:"payload"`
	Bindings    []Binding `json:"bindings,omitempty"`
	Unfocusable bool      `json:"unfocusable,omitempty"`
	Label       string    `json:"label,omitempty"` // a display name (a list row's label, a header name); {{label}} in a confirm prompt
	Data        any       `json:"data,omitempty"`  // an opaque application value (a list row's ListItem.Data)
	Pin         string    `json:"pin,omitempty"`   // the declared base mode (PinInstance)
	Component   any       `json:"-"`
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}
Source file

InstanceOption configures the instance constructors: Instance, AddPage,

type InstanceOption func(*instanceSpec)

JobLinger constant
JobLinger is how long a finished job stays on the ticker after the
frame that first saw it finished. The Model schedules one JobLingerMsg
for the earliest remembered finish (Model.lingerCommand), so the row
goes on an idle screen; any earlier frame past the deadline drops it
too, and a newer job can push it off sooner.

const JobLinger = 4 * time.Second
Source file
JobLingerMsg type
JobLingerMsg is the ticker's one-shot timer: it fires when the oldest
remembered finished job passes JobLinger, so the frame it causes drops
the row with no key pressed. At is the deadline it was scheduled for;
only the message for the timer in flight clears it.

type JobLingerMsg struct{ At time.Time }

Layout type
Layout renders page chrome, bounded panels and panel pairs for a viewport.
It is a plain value: every method only renders and keeps no state.

type Layout struct {
	Width, Height int
	Styles        Styles
	// Shift moves the divider of every split this layout draws, in percent
	// points of the split's length: positive grows the first (left or top)
	// panel, negative the second, 0 is the split's default. It is the
	// page's remembered divider (Runtime.PaneLayout sets it; pane.grow,
	// pane.shrink and pane.reset change it). Divide applies it.
	Shift int
	// Only draws the focused panel of a split alone, filling the body
	// (pane.only; Runtime.PaneLayout sets it): SplitFocusBlock with a
	// focused side draws that panel, SplitBlock and Split the left one;
	// with SplitNone both are drawn.
	Only bool
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}

Layout.Divide function / method
Divide is where a split of total cells puts its divider: first is the
split's default size of its first (left or top) panel, moved by Shift
percent points of total and clamped so each panel keeps least cells. A
split with no shift, or too small for two panels of least cells, keeps
first. A layout of its own (a page body split by hand) calls it with its
default; Split and its kin call it for theirs.

func (l Layout) Divide(first, total, least int) int
Source file
Layout.HeaderRule function / method
HeaderRule is the rule line under the header, width cells: the plain
rule with no segments; with segments, rule characters, then the
segments right-aligned, joined by " · ", then " ──". Segments are
dropped from the last while they do not fit, and when the first alone
does not, it is cut with "…"; at least "── " of rule stays on the
left. Each segment draws in its tone (muted without one), the rule and
separators muted, so the chrome still reads as a rule.

func (l Layout) HeaderRule(cells []SegmentCell, width int) string

raw label. Content that does not fit is clipped and marked with "…" (columns)

LineScroller type
LineScroller is a component that windows its own lines and scrolls that
window without a motion of the cursor (Vim's ctrl+e and ctrl+y, :help
CTRL-E): the window moves, and the cursor stays where it is unless it
would leave the window (with the page's scrolloff), when it moves to the
nearest line still shown. A list (tables and trees through their list),
a timeline and a markdown document implement it; view.scroll scrolls the
innermost one on the focus path that can.

type LineScroller interface {
	// CanScrollLines reports whether the last frame windowed the lines (more
	// lines than the window shows), so a scroll would move it.
	CanScrollLines() bool
	// ScrollLines moves the window by lines, down when positive, clamped to
	// the first and last line.
	ScrollLines(lines int)
}

List.Patch function / method
Patch applies a record-level change (ListPatch) without the caller
rebuilding the rows: it is SetItems over the patched rows, so a row that
stays keeps its instance (and the bindings and pending jobs on it), the
selection and the marks follow their ids, and only a removed row's
instance is closed. An empty patch does nothing; an invalid one (an
index out of order or out of range, a repeated id) leaves the list
unchanged. The patch applies to every row, the ones a filter hides
included, and SetItems filters the result again.

func (l *List) Patch(p ListPatch) error

List.SetItems function / method
SetItems copies rows, preserves selection by ID, and falls back to the nearest
remaining index if that ID disappears. Invalid updates leave the list unchanged.
A filter (SetFilter) stays and applies to the new rows.
It creates one instance per new row under the list and removes the instances
of rows that disappear; a retained row keeps its instance and any bindings
the application put on it. Rows whose cells are unchanged keep a table's
measured widths. A record-level change is Patch.

Source file
ListItem type
ListItem is application-owned identity, display text, and the registered
noun the row answers to. Each row is an Instance whose ID is the item ID and
whose Parent is the list; its bindings and actions come from its noun.
Data is an opaque value the application keeps on the row (a host's
record of the row, an extension's own id); SetItems copies it to the
row instance's Data, where a verb reads it as Ctx.Instance.Data. The
framework never reads it. Cells are the row's text per column when the
list has columns (a table, Columns); Label stays the row's name for the
palette, search and {{label}}, and is the first cell of a row without
cells. Section groups a table's rows: List.View draws the section as a
label line above the first row of each run of rows with the same
Section (a list without columns ignores it). Tones give cells, or runs
inside them, a named tone the theme colours (ToneSpan, tone.go; in a
list without columns cell 0 is the label); they never change the text.

type ListItem struct {
	ID    string `json:"id"`
	Label string `json:"label"`
	Noun  string `json:"noun"`
	Data  any    `json:"data,omitempty"`
	// Props supplies the row's noun prop values when Data is an opaque handle.
	// Nil reads props from Data via ThingOf instead.
	Props   Thing      `json:"props,omitempty"`
	Cells   []string   `json:"cells,omitempty"`
	Section string     `json:"section,omitempty"`
	Tones   []ToneSpan `json:"tones,omitempty"`
	// Classes are application-defined labels for list.next-class and previous-class.
	Classes []string `json:"classes,omitempty"`
	// Search is more text the / filter matches the row by, beside its
	// label and cells, never drawn: what the row stands for (the Settings
	// page's section row holds its settings' names, descriptions, values,
	// defaults and choices).
	Search []string `json:"search,omitempty"`
}
Source file
ListNoSpillSetting constant
ListNoSpillSetting is list.no-spill: the ids of lists, tables, trees
and timelines whose motions clamp at their edges as NoSpill() does (the
per-user opt-out from the spill, UX-RULES R6.12). An entry ending in *
is a prefix; an entry may end in one edge (ID:last, ID:first,
ID:first-col, ID:last-col; SpillEdges), else it traps all four.

const ListNoSpillSetting = "list.no-spill"
Source file
ListPatch type
ListPatch is a record-level change to a list's rows: the rows that
changed or were added, whole (Upsert), and the ids of the rows that went
away (Remove). Every other row stays as it is, in its order.

At, when given, is each upserted row's index in the patched list, one per
Upsert and ascending: the rows neither upserted nor removed keep their
relative order and fill the indexes the upserts leave free. Without At a
row whose id the list has replaces it in place and a new row is
appended, in Upsert order.

type ListPatch struct {
	Upsert []ListItem
	At     []int
	Remove []string
}
Source file
ListPatch.Empty function / method
Empty reports whether the patch changes nothing.

func (p ListPatch) Empty() bool
Source file
ListQuery type
ListQuery is what list hands its store: the where pairs (prop to typed
value, equality, all of them), the limit (0 for none) and the store's
own ListParams the request gave. A store may use them to read less; the
runtime applies where and limit to the answer either way, so a store
that ignores them is still right.

type ListQuery struct {
	Where map[string]any
	Limit int
	Args  Arguments
}

Model type
Model is the Bubble Tea adapter. Pages and modal instances retain their
state in Runtime; components (picker, registers, application components)
are instances the runtime drives through the delivery contract, so the
adapter holds no component list of its own.

type Model struct {
	// Foreground answers a ForegroundMsg (a verb's Foreground program);
	// nil runs it through tea.ExecProcess, suspending the TUI. o-story
	// and tests replace it to run the program headless.
	Foreground func(ForegroundMsg) tea.Cmd
	Runtime    *Runtime
	Palette    *Palette
	Appearance *Appearance
	ModalViews map[string]ModalView
	Title      string
	Width      int
	Height     int
	// Status is the status line in its error tone: the last key's error,
	// or what a handler set with ctx.Error (ctx.Status); the footer reads
	// "Error: TEXT" in the error style in place of the hints. Info is the
	// line in its neutral tone (ctx.Info), drawn plain before the hints.
	// Setting one clears the other, and the next key clears both.
	Status string
	Info   string
	// Notices are persistent lines (a host's mount warnings), kept across
	// keys and errors until notice.clear (or the application) clears them.
	// The footer shows the newest before the hints, cut to half the width,
	// with "(+N more)" for the rest (notice.list has them all), so notices
	// never hide the hints. A notice does not go with the next key. The
	// Model is the runtime's UI sink (Runtime.UI), so ctx.Notify,
	// ctx.Error and ctx.Info land here.
	Notices []string

	// Sidebar replaces page tabs with a navigation rail. Existing page operations
	// remain the only navigation state; input focus and modal routing are unchanged.
	Sidebar bool

	// Now is the Model's clock: the ticker's memory of finished jobs and
	// the linger timer read it (time.Now by default, time.Now when nil). A
	// headless host fakes it, advances it to JobLingerTick's deadline and
	// delivers that message instead of waiting JobLinger.
	Now func() time.Time
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}

Model.Init function / method
Init installs the user actions file (Runtime.EnsureUserActions) and
applies the user bindings file (Runtime.EnsureUserBindings), after the
application defined its own nouns and rows, declares the framework's settings
(EnsureConfigSettings), reads settings.json (its problems are notices),
draws themes.json's schemes and the theme setting's (applyThemeSetting),
restores and persists the action history (EnsureHistory), leaves a start
page the pages.hidden setting hides for the first page in the tabs
(OpenVisibleStart), starts the settings.json poll (SettingsPoll) when the
application declared settings, and asks for the background colour.

Model.JobLingerTick function / method
JobLingerTick is the linger timer in flight, the message its tea.Tick
will deliver at its deadline (At), and false when none is scheduled. A
host with a fake Now advances the clock to At and delivers the message
itself rather than waiting JobLinger.

It is the Model's one linger timer: the key echo's entries (keys.echo-
linger) ride it too, so the earlier of the two deadlines in flight is the
one returned, and its message clears whichever timer it was for.

func (m *Model) JobLingerTick() (JobLingerMsg, bool)

Noun type
Noun is the behaviour shared by every instance of one kind: the verbs it
answers to, its key bindings per mode, the action handlers that override an
operation's default while the noun is on the focus stack, and an optional
pinned mode (the prompt noun pins insert). Focusable marks every instance
of the noun as a focus stop (focus.next and the mode rule read it); Region
marks a grouping that focus.next {scope: "region"} jumps between.

Bindings is keyed by mode name. "" holds rows that apply in every base
mode; a pending mode (operator_pending, a key-sequence prefix, ...) is
exclusive and reads only its own table.

type Noun struct {
	Name      string               `json:"name"`
	About     string               `json:"about,omitempty"`
	Verbs     []string             `json:"verbs,omitempty"`
	Pin       string               `json:"pin,omitempty"`
	Focusable bool                 `json:"focusable,omitempty"`
	Region    bool                 `json:"region,omitempty"`
	Bindings  map[string][]Binding `json:"bindings"`
	Extends   map[string]string    `json:"extends,omitempty"`
	Guards    map[string]string    `json:"guards,omitempty"`
	// Availability are the guards added to a verb beside its own
	// (Runtime.AddVerbAvailability), by verb: every one must hold too.
	Availability map[string][]string `json:"availability,omitempty"`
	Actions      map[string]Handler  `json:"-"`
	// Columns are the table columns a list of the noun's rows draws
	// (NounBuilder.Columns, the catalog's columns): declared data a host
	// passes to Columns when it builds the list, never applied on its own.
	Columns []Column `json:"columns,omitempty"`
	// Completions are the completion triggers of the noun's inputs that
	// declare none of their own (NounBuilder.Completions).
	Completions []CompletionTrigger `json:"-"`
	// Props are the fields of the noun's things (NounBuilder.Props,
	// props.go), in declaration order; Store is its store row
	// (NounBuilder.Store), nil when it declares none.
	Props []PropSpec `json:"props,omitempty"`
	Store *Store     `json:"-"`
	// Filters are default saved prop filters for lists of this noun's rows.
	Filters []SavedFilter `json:"filters,omitempty"`
	// PropsOff are fields of the noun's rows that prop filters never offer
	// (NounBuilder.PropsOff, the catalog's propsOff): a field name or a
	// path.Match pattern over it. Every other field is filterable.
	PropsOff []string `json:"propsOff,omitempty"`
	// Classes declare the row classes and their optional motion keys.
	Classes []ClassDecl `json:"classes,omitempty"`
}

NounPlace type
NounPlace is one place a noun was seen: an instance of Noun registered
under instances of the Ancestors nouns (outermost first, app left out),
on Page, or inside the open modal Modal. Inside a modal the ancestors
start at the modal instance, where the stack walk stops; Page is then the
page the modal opened over. Instance ids are never part of a placement
(rows and generated instances differ per run), only nouns and the page id
(page ids are stable). A list row counts once per SetItems for each noun
its rows answer to, not once per row.

type NounPlace struct {
	Noun      string    `json:"noun"`
	Ancestors []string  `json:"ancestors"`
	Page      string    `json:"page,omitempty"`
	PageTitle string    `json:"pageTitle,omitempty"`
	Modal     string    `json:"modal,omitempty"`
	Count     int       `json:"count"`
	First     time.Time `json:"first"`
	Last      time.Time `json:"last"`
}
