Source file
Operation type
Operation is one registered noun.verb. Kind is "interaction" (the
default: Run executes synchronously on the loop) or "application" (App is
an AppHandler whose pending work runs off the loop and reports through the
delivery contract). Confirm, when set, is a prompt template ({{id}},
{{noun}}, {{label}}, {{<arg>}}) shown in a confirm modal before the operation runs.
Repeatable marks a change (not a motion or navigation) that dot-repeat
(history.repeat, bound to . on app in normal mode) may run again; set it
on the Operation passed to Register.

type Operation struct {
	Destructive bool      `json:"destructive,omitempty"`
	Agent       string    `json:"agent,omitempty"`
	Examples    []Example `json:"examples,omitempty"`
	// CatalogGuards preserves a proxy's remote guards; the remote runtime checks them.
	// CatalogProxy marks a verb forwarded to an extension runtime.
	CatalogProxy  bool                 `json:"-"`
	CatalogGuards []string             `json:"-"`
	Verb          string               `json:"verb"`
	Noun          string               `json:"noun"`
	Description   string               `json:"description"`
	Parameters    map[string]Parameter `json:"parameters"`
	Result        string               `json:"result"`
	Kind          string               `json:"kind"`
	Confirm       string               `json:"confirm,omitempty"`
	Repeatable    bool                 `json:"repeatable,omitempty"`
	// Rows names the noun whose rows the verb's result lists (a list
	// verb's JSON array of rows of that noun); "" when the result is not
	// rows. It is data for hosts (the catalog's rows); the runtime does
	// not read it.
	Rows string `json:"rows,omitempty"`
	// State marks the verb whose answer is the rows of every one of a
	// host's lists in one call (NounBuilder.State), as JSON Lines of
	// cli.Row tagged by list noun ({"noun":"agent","id":..,"label":..}).
	// Data for hosts (the catalog's state); the runtime does not read it.
	State bool `json:"state,omitempty"`
	// Quiet marks bookkeeping (a list reload) the jobs ticker does not
	// show and the inspector does not retain as the last user request;
	// set by NounBuilder.Quiet. The catalog's quiet.
	Quiet bool `json:"quiet,omitempty"`
	// Builtin marks an operation the framework registered itself: every
	// one NewRuntime and NewModel register, and those a framework
	// constructor registers later (NewPicker, NewPalette, grid.Register,
	// markdown.Register, cli.AddCommands), each inside Runtime.Builtin. An
	// application's verb is never builtin, even on a framework noun
	// (history.capture-hook). Data for hosts (the catalog's builtin): a
	// host mounting another tool's verbs skips these, which every
	// ochazuke tool has; the runtime does not read it.
	Builtin bool `json:"builtin,omitempty"`
	// PersistArgs lets the history file keep this operation's arguments
	// named like text (text, value, keys, args, password, secret, token), which
	// it otherwise leaves out; set by NounBuilder.PersistArgs. Only for an
	// operation whose arguments are never typed secrets (settings.set).
	PersistArgs bool `json:"persistArgs,omitempty"`
	// Motion marks movement (list.move, focus.next, surface.scroll,
	// page.next): the footer's hint line leaves its rows out, since motions
	// are standard and the footer is for the verbs the focus offers; ?
	// help and bindings list keep them. Set by NounBuilder.Motion; the
	// framework's own motions are marked as they register (motionOps).
	Motion bool `json:"motion,omitempty"`
	// Typing marks an operation that passes the key that ran it on as
	// content rather than acting on it as a command: typing into an input
	// (input.insert), keys forwarded to a pane (terminal.send,
	// terminal.paste). The key echo (keys.echo) collapses a run of them
	// into the text typed, and hides the ones a passthrough mode forwards
	// unless keys.echo-terminal is on. The framework's own are marked as
	// they register (typingOps).
	Typing bool `json:"typing,omitempty"`
	// HistoryKind is the kind of entry the operation records
	// (HistoryAction, HistoryChange, HistoryFetch); empty derives it
	// (HistoryKindOf). Set it to HistoryFetch on a verb that only asks for
	// data (NounBuilder.Fetch, the catalog's history), so history.list's
	// default view and the bounded ring leave its calls to the fetches.
	HistoryKind string `json:"historyKind,omitempty"`
	// Positional names the parameters bare words fill first, in this
	// order (NounBuilder.Positional), ahead of ParameterOrder's
	// required-then-by-name rest. The CLI gives a boolean a bare word only
	// when it is named here. The catalog's positional.
	Positional []string `json:"positional,omitempty"`
	// Many marks a verb whose handler takes every subject of a request at
	// once (Ctx.Subjects: a visual selection's rows), so it asks one
	// question, makes one change and posts one notice for all of them; set
	// by NounBuilder.Many. A verb without it that is asked to act on
	// several subjects runs once per subject. The catalog's many.
	Many bool `json:"many,omitempty"`
	// Aliases are the action's other names (ActionAlias), display
	// spelling, sorted. Catalog and operations.list fill it; an entry of
	// Runtime.Operations does not hold it (the alias table does).
	Aliases []string `json:"aliases,omitempty"`
	// Standard is the standard verb (standard_verbs.go: get, list) the
	// runtime generated this operation from for a noun with a store, ""
	// for every verb written by hand. An application verb of the same key
	// replaces a standard one, registered before or after. The catalog's
	// standard.
	Standard string `json:"standard,omitempty"`
	// Checks run before the verb from every path (AddVerbCheck,
	// BeforeQuit, verb_checks.go): each allows, asks first or refuses.
	Checks []VerbCheck `json:"-"`
	// ConfirmFor, when set, is the prompt template one request asks in
	// place of Confirm (given as prompt), read when the request would ask:
	// a verb whose confirm depends on what it will do to the thing (the
	// standard delete under delete.archive). "" asks nothing.
	ConfirmFor func(r *Runtime, c Context, q Request, prompt string) string `json:"-"`
	Run        Handler                                                      `json:"-"`
	App        AppHandler                                                   `json:"-"`
}

OperatorWritableGuard constant
OperatorWritableGuard is input.operator's: false while the focus is a
read-only input and the request applies an operator that changes the
text to the visual selection (a visual mode's d, c, x: no motion), so
those rows stand aside and leave the hints. An operator over a motion
(d w, d d) stays available, and the handler leaves it a no-op; the
editor's normal c and d rows carry input-writable as their row guard,
so a read-only input never enters operator pending for them and the
hint line does not offer them (y still does).

const OperatorWritableGuard = "input-operator-writable"

Origin type
Origin is where a setting's value comes from, as :verbose names it.
Layer is OriginDefault, OriginFile or OriginSession. For a default,
Path is the embedded settings file (settings/ochazuke.json) or the
application's file:line that declared it, and By is "ochazuke" or
"app". For the file, Path is settings.json and Line the line of its
key (0 when not found). For a session value, Source is the history
source of the request that set it ("palette", "key:<keys>", "cli",
"api", "macro:<r>"), Via the name it ran by (an alias such as
setting.save, or the action's key) and At the time. Disk, when set, is
when the poll (or settings.reload) read another value for the setting
from settings.json: another process changed it under this runtime.

type Origin struct {
	Layer  string    `json:"layer"`
	Path   string    `json:"path,omitempty"`
	Line   int       `json:"line,omitempty"`
	By     string    `json:"by,omitempty"`
	Source string    `json:"source,omitempty"`
	Via    string    `json:"via,omitempty"`
	At     time.Time `json:"at,omitzero"`
	Disk   time.Time `json:"disk,omitzero"`
}

when the file moved under the runtime.

func (o Origin) String() string

OverlayMark is plain text drawn from one cell of the frame (Row and Col,

Source file
OwnerFramework, OwnerApp, SettingsGeneralSection constant
Owners and sections: where the Settings page files each setting. A
setting's owner (Setting.Owner, resolved by Settings.OwnerOf) is a tab of
the page; its section (Setting.SectionName) is a row of the tab's section
list; its group (Setting.Group) a heading inside the section. Which owner,
section and group a setting has comes only from these fields: no code
looks at a setting's name beyond the first-dot rule of SectionName.

SettingOwner and SettingSection declarations give an owner or a section
its title, a one-line description and its place in the order; without
one the title is the ID and the order is the order the owner's or the
section's first setting was declared. The framework's own declarations
are the embedded settings/sections.json; an application adds its own with
Runtime.DeclareSettingOwner and Runtime.DeclareSettingSection.

const (
	// OwnerFramework owns the framework's settings: the embedded rows and
	// what a framework feature declares (inside Runtime.Builtin).
	OwnerFramework = FrameworkNamespace
	// OwnerApp owns a setting that names no owner: the declaring
	// application's. Its title, unless declared, is Config.App.
	OwnerApp = AliasApp
	// SettingsGeneralSection is the section of a setting whose name has
	// no dot and that names no section.
	SettingsGeneralSection = "general"
)
Source file
Page type
Page is the component a page instance fronts: a title, the instance it
starts focused on (Focus), the focus it remembers while another page is
open (Remembered, restored with its mode by OpenPage), and a renderer. Render returns the
page body for the given cell bounds as a Block; its cursor, when the page
holds the focused input, is relative to the body's top-left cell. Pages
without inputs return Text(body).

type Page struct {
	ID         string                         `json:"id"`
	Title      string                         `json:"title"`
	Focus      string                         `json:"focus"`
	Remembered string                         `json:"remembered"`
	Render     func(*Runtime, int, int) Block `json:"-"`
	// OnOpen, when set, runs on the loop each time OpenPage makes this page
	// current from another page (or from none: the first page opened), after
	// the focus and mode are restored; from is the page left, "" for none.
	// Reopening the current page does not run it. An application refreshes
	// what the page shows here (SetItems, or Launch a reload job owned by
	// one of its instances) instead of polling. Every way a page opens goes
	// through OpenPage: page.open, page.next/previous, ctrl+j/k, the page
	// picker.
	OnOpen func(r *Runtime, from string) `json:"-"`
	// Scrolloff is how many lines the page keeps visible above and below
	// the focused element when it scrolls to follow the focus: 0 means the
	// default, 2; a negative value keeps no margin.
	Scrolloff int `json:"scrolloff,omitempty"`
	// DefaultHidden declares the page hidden by default: an opt-in page,
	// out of the tabs and ctrl+j/k until page.show brings it in. It is
	// data for the pages.hidden setting's default (PagesHiddenSetting);
	// the setting, once the user sets it, decides. Set it before
	// RegisterPage, or pass the DefaultHidden option.
	DefaultHidden bool `json:"defaultHidden,omitempty"`
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}
Source file
PagesHiddenSetting constant
PagesHiddenSetting is the framework setting that hides pages from the
tab row and the page-cycling keys (page.next, page.previous, ctrl+j/k):
a list of page ids. A hidden page stays registered and reachable:
page.open, the page picker (marked hidden), the palette, the CLI and an
application's own OpenPage still open it, and while it is current its
tab shows so the chrome says where the focus is. Its default is the ids
of the pages declared hidden by default (Page.DefaultHidden, the
DefaultHidden option, a catalog page's defaultHidden), so an
application's opt-in pages start hidden. Once settings.json holds a
value it is the whole list; settings reset pages.hidden goes back to the
application's defaults. It is read at every frame and every page motion,
so settings set, page.hide, page.show, config.reload and another
process's write apply on the next one.

const PagesHiddenSetting = "pages.hidden"

ParamAlias type
ParamAlias is an old name of a parameter, taken by the action alias
that declares it (Keep actions, for a parameter: a renamed flag works
for one release). Alias is the old name, Param the parameter of the
alias's action it fills. Since and RemoveAfter date a deprecated one:
its first use in a runtime posts a one-time notice naming the new name.
An old name is read from the CLI (--starred), the palette (--starred),
binding rows' args and the API alike; it is never completed and help
never lists it. The parameter's own name wins when both are given.

type ParamAlias struct {
	Alias       string `json:"alias"`
	Param       string `json:"param"`
	Since       string `json:"since,omitempty"`
	RemoveAfter string `json:"removeAfter,omitempty"`
	Note        string `json:"note,omitempty"`
}

Parameter type
Parameter describes one argument of an operation: its type, whether it is required, and its choices.

type Parameter struct {
	Type        string `json:"type"`
	Required    bool   `json:"required,omitempty"`
	Description string `json:"description,omitempty"`
	// Enum lists the static choices of a string parameter; Source names an
	// operation (noun.verb) whose result lists more. Free also accepts a
	// value outside them.
	Enum   []string `json:"enum,omitempty"`
	Source string   `json:"source,omitempty"`
	Free   bool     `json:"free,omitempty"`
	// Noun says the value names one of that noun's things: a row of its
	// list verb (NOUN.list, when it is a read with no required parameter,
	// stands in for an empty Source), else the id of a live instance of
	// the noun. A Source still wins; Enum values come first.
	Noun string `json:"noun,omitempty"`
	// A path parameter (Type "path") names a file, or a folder when Path
	// is "dir"; its form field opens the path chooser (path_chooser.go),
	// whose browse starts at Base (~ and relative to the working
	// directory) when the field is empty. New allows a path that does not
	// exist yet (a file to write).
	Path string `json:"path,omitempty"`
	Base string `json:"base,omitempty"`
	New  bool   `json:"new,omitempty"`
	// Rest marks a string parameter that takes the rest of the line,
	// verbatim: the palette hands it the text from its first word to the
	// end, quotes and spaces as typed, and the CLI joins the remaining
	// words with spaces. A verb has at most one, the last its words fill
	// (settings.apply's line: :set scrolloff=5 nowrap). Its Source is asked
	// with the text as the query and, when it declares
	// ChoicesCursorParameter, the cursor in it; each choice's id is the
	// text up to the cursor with the completion in place.
	Rest bool `json:"rest,omitempty"`
	// Focus declares that an omitted value is filled from the focus: the
	// instance the request acts for once ExecuteAt has applied its target
	// and the focus path (Ctx.Instance, or the nearest instance of Noun
	// above it). A standard verb's subject parameter (standard_verbs.go)
	// declares it, and so does a create parameter of a FromFocus prop;
	// values never come from the focus unless their parameter says so,
	// and a fill is always shown (the palette slot, the args form). The
	// catalog's focus.
	Focus bool `json:"focus,omitempty"`
	// Edits names the prop of the subject this parameter writes (rename's
	// name writes the label prop): the args form opens holding the prop's
	// current value, selected as a rename is (rename.select), or, when it
	// is empty, empty with the prop's Fallback value as its placeholder.
	Edits string `json:"edits,omitempty"`
	// ValueOf names the parameter whose argument is a prop of the
	// operation's noun: this parameter holds a value of that prop, so its
	// choices (and the palette's check of a typed word) are the prop's own
	// (its Enum, Source or Noun; true and false for a boolean). A standard
	// set's value declares ValueOf "prop". The catalog's valueOf.
	ValueOf string `json:"valueOf,omitempty"`
	// Pairs marks a parameter that takes NAME=VALUE words of the noun's
	// props (where, set's several-prop form): a word holding = completes
	// the values of prop NAME; one without completes the Enum when there
	// is one, else NAME= for each prop. The catalog's pairs.
	Pairs bool `json:"pairs,omitempty"`
	// Excludes names the parameters a request cannot give with this one
	// (set's toggle and value). A preset (an action alias's args) that a
	// caller's argument excludes, or that excludes it, yields to the
	// caller's (Operation.WithPresets). The catalog's excludes.
	Excludes []string `json:"excludes,omitempty"`
}

ParseSchemes function / method
ParseSchemes reads colour schemes in the themes.json shape, an object of
schemes by name:

	{"kombu": {"background": "dark", "accent": "#C9A227",
	           "error": {"light": "#B3261E", "dark": "#FF8A80"},
	           "tones": {"state.working": {"fg": "#5FAFD7", "bold": true}}},
	 "dark":  {"tones": {"build.cached": "muted"}}}

A role left out takes the built-in palette's; "tones" (ToneStyle by
tone name) are laid over the built-in tones. An entry named after a
built-in theme holds only "tones": they override and add to that
theme's (adaptive's reach dark and light too). A scheme that does not
validate (an unknown role, a bad colour, background or tone, a built-in's
name with anything but tones, an error colour equal to the accent or
muted one) is skipped and reported; a file that is not a JSON object
yields no schemes.

func ParseSchemes(data []byte) (map[string]Scheme, []string)

Payload type
Payload is the small typed state a mode carries (Runtime.Mode.Payload).
For a pending mode Return is the mode it returns to on leave.
Count and OpCount are the Vim counts before and after an operator; Op is
the pending operator; Prefix is the key sequence typed so far; Request is a
stored request (the completed match a sequence prefix falls back to, or a
confirm's deferred action); Prompt is the text a confirm modal shows;
Shadow is the mode the macro_recording shadow mode re-dispatches in.

type Payload struct {
	Return  string     `json:"return,omitempty"`
	Count   int        `json:"count,omitempty"`
	OpCount int        `json:"opCount,omitempty"`
	Op      string     `json:"op,omitempty"`
	Prefix  string     `json:"prefix,omitempty"`
	Prompt  string     `json:"prompt,omitempty"`
	Request *Request   `json:"request,omitempty"`
	Shadow  *ModeState `json:"shadow,omitempty"`
	// Anchor is the instance a modal is opened for (a form field, for its
	// chooser): the modal is drawn beside it (modal.anchor).
	Anchor string `json:"anchor,omitempty"`
}

Picker type
Picker owns one modal and one ordinary editor. Background commands only read
immutable snapshots; HandleMessage is the sole result-application boundary.
Its work runs as four replacing jobs on the picker instance (the "index",
"search", "preview" and "stat" slots), so the runtime's jobs list decides which
result is current: a newer job in a slot drops the older, and Close
cancels them all.

type Picker struct {
	Runtime   *Runtime
	Origin    Context
	Directory string
	Filter    string
	Entries   []PickerEntry
	Selected  int
	Error     string
	Loading   bool
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}

Predicate type
Predicate is one named test over an instance: a value, never a string
selector. A query is a list of predicates, ANDed. Name is a registered
predicate (noun, id, kind, within, child-of, focusable, region, state or
one an application adds) and Arg its argument; the combinators any, all
and not take nested predicate lists in Of instead. The wire form is a JSON
array: ["noun","row"], ["any",[["noun","a"],["noun","b"]]].

type Predicate struct {
	Name string      `json:"name"`
	Arg  string      `json:"arg,omitempty"`
	Of   []Predicate `json:"of,omitempty"`
}

PropFilter type
PropFilter is one typed condition on a noun's props. Conditions combine
with AND. Op is = (also the default), contains (literal substring, or an
item of a list prop), in (Value is an array of alternatives), or one of
their negations: !=, not-contains, not-in (filterOps). Equality uses the
same typed comparison as the standard list verb's --where. A row without
the prop matches no condition, a negated one included.

type PropFilter struct {
	Prop  string `json:"prop"`
	Op    string `json:"op,omitempty"`
	Value any    `json:"value"`
}

func PropShorthand(noun, prop string, value any, toggle bool, args Arguments) Request
Source file
PropSpec type
PropSpec is one field of a noun's things. Parameter carries the type,
Required, Enum, Source, Noun and Description, as a verb parameter does,
so the palette, CLI help and forms read it unchanged; Type is a setting
type or PropObject ("" is string). A prop whose Parameter.Noun is set is
a reference: its value is the key of one of that noun's things.

ReadOnly props are never written by set (a Key prop always is);
CreateOnly ones are written by create only. Role gives the prop a part
(RoleLabel, RoleOwner). Fallback names another prop of the noun shown in
this prop's place when this one is empty: display only (list rows,
{{label}}, choosers); get still reads the prop itself. Default is what
create writes when the value is omitted. A Secret prop is left out of
get's and list's records unless asked for by name, and its writes are
never repeated or persisted. Multiline asks a form for a text area.
FromFocus, on a reference prop, lets create fill it from the focused
thing of its noun when the request omits it (the create.from-focus
setting, on by default; the fill is always shown: the palette slot and
the args form hold it, and create's notice names it). Values are never
read from the focus unless their prop says so.
When names a guard: where it is false (read where the request acts) the
prop is not offered, read or written (the catalog's availability); the
guard is the prop's own, apart from the noun's verb guards.

Prop filters (list_props.go) offer every field of a list's rows, declared
or not. NoFilter turns a declared prop off for filtering (a Secret prop
never is offered). Derived marks a spec the framework read from row data
for a field the noun does not declare: it is ReadOnly and lives only in
the list's filter layer, never on the noun, so set, forms, the palette,
the CLI and MCP cannot write it. Every field is filterable; only declared
fields are writable.

type PropSpec struct {
	Name string `json:"name"`
	Parameter
	ReadOnly   bool   `json:"readOnly,omitempty"`
	CreateOnly bool   `json:"createOnly,omitempty"`
	Key        bool   `json:"key,omitempty"`
	Role       string `json:"role,omitempty"`
	Fallback   string `json:"fallback,omitempty"`
	FromFocus  bool   `json:"fromFocus,omitempty"`
	Default    any    `json:"default,omitempty"`
	Secret     bool   `json:"secret,omitempty"`
	Multiline  bool   `json:"multiline,omitempty"`
	When       string `json:"availability,omitempty"`
	NoFilter   bool   `json:"noFilter,omitempty"`
	Derived    bool   `json:"derived,omitempty"`
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}

RecordEditor type
RecordEditor is a form over one record: a field per schema entry, each a
focus stop, and every change written as it is made. Typing in a text
field is written after a pause (Debounce) and at once when the field is
left or insert mode ends; a choice, an edit in normal mode, an undo or a
redo is written at once. A value that is not valid (Required, the type,
the choices, Validate) is not written, and its label says why. u and
ctrl+r undo and redo the record's written changes, each written like any
other; q and esc leave, and nothing is ever discarded. There is no save
key.

The editor is an instance of the form noun (id) with a child per field
(id/name): text fields are inputs of form-field (form-text when
multiline), choice fields instances of form-choice, whose enter opens the
chooser (a modal list of form-option rows).

type RecordEditor struct {

	// Styles colour the labels (the zero value draws them plain); a modal
	// editor uses the Model's.
	Styles Styles
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}
Source file

Block draws the form: per field its label (with why a value is not
written) and its value, a text field as its input, a choice field as its
value with the one highlight while focused; a failed write is the last
line. Each field is placed in drawing order, so motions follow it.

func (e *RecordEditor) Block(width, height int) Block

RecordField type
RecordField is one editable field of a record schema. Its Parameter is
the same description a verb's parameter carries (Type, Required, Enum,
Source, Noun, Free, Description), so a form can come from a verb's
catalog parameters (RecordFields). A field with choices (Enum, Source or
Noun: Runtime.Choices) and not Free, or of Type boolean, is edited
through a chooser, never as free text. A Free field with choices is a
text field with suggestions (form-suggest):
typing stays free text, enter in normal mode or ctrl+space opens the
framework chooser over the suggestions (the Enum, then the Source's
result, loaded off the loop when the Source verb is Pending), a pick
fills the field and is written at once, and a value outside the
suggestions is written as typed; it is one line high (Multiline is
ignored). The rest are text inputs, Multiline ones several lines high.

type RecordField struct {
	Name  string `json:"name"`
	Label string `json:"label,omitempty"` // shown above the value (default Name)
	Parameter
	Multiline bool `json:"multiline,omitempty"`
	// Secret makes the field's input secret (Input.Secret): what is typed
	// into it is never echoed. PropField sets it from a Secret prop.
	Secret bool `json:"secret,omitempty"`
	// Placeholder is shown, muted, in the field while its text is empty
	// (a rename's Fallback: what the thing shows with no name).
	Placeholder string `json:"placeholder,omitempty"`
	// Validate checks a value the type, Required and the choices allow;
	// its error is why the value is not written.
	Validate func(value string) error `json:"-"`
	// Chips makes a field with choices take several of them: its value is
	// a JSON array of choice ids ("" for none), drawn as chips wrapped
	// across the lines under its label (record_chips.go).
	Chips bool `json:"chips,omitempty"`
	// Invertible gives the field a normal and an inverted state
	// (form.invert); the record carries it as the field's name plus
	// RecordInvertSuffix, "true" while inverted.
	Invertible bool `json:"invertible,omitempty"`
	// Completions complete a text field's tokens as it is typed
	// (completion.go): ctrl+n and ctrl+p walk the suggestions.
	Completions []CompletionTrigger `json:"-"`
}
Source file

func RecordFields(op Operation) []RecordField

Source file
RecordSpec type
RecordSpec is what an application gives a record editor: the schema, how
to load the record and how to write it.

type RecordSpec struct {
	Title  string // the modal's title; a placed editor's heading is the application's
	Fields []RecordField
	// Load reads the record when the editor opens (nil: every field empty).
	Load func() (Record, error)
	// Save writes one change: Done, Failed, or Pending work that runs off
	// the loop. At most one write is in flight; changes made meanwhile go
	// in the next write. A failed write keeps the values on screen, says
	// so, and is retried with the next change.
	Save func(RecordWrite) Result
	// Debounce is the pause in typing before a text field is written
	// (DefaultRecordDebounce when zero).
	Debounce time.Duration
	// Insert opens the editor with the first field in insert mode when it
	// is a text field.
	Insert bool
	// Rename says the editor renames a thing: it opens in insert like
	// Insert, and with the start field's text selected (Input.SelectAll)
	// while the rename.select setting is on, so typing replaces the name
	// and a motion keeps it.
	Rename bool
	// OnLeave runs after the editor closed (form.leave, or form.run's
	// Submit): every change is written or queued by then; a placed editor
	// stops being drawn.
	OnLeave func()
	// Submit, when set, makes the editor a form that runs once instead of
	// writing each change: Save is never called, changes stay on screen,
	// and form.run (available once every field is valid) closes a modal
	// editor and hands Submit every value as shown. A failed Submit
	// reopens the modal with the values and the error (`! not run:`);
	// leaving (form.leave, esc) runs nothing.
	Submit func(Record) Result
	// Start names the field focused first ("" the first field).
	Start string
	// Clearable offers form.clear: every field emptied and every inverted
	// field back to normal, each written (and undone) like any change.
	Clearable bool
	// Watch names the noun whose store holds the record: when its things
	// change other than through this editor (a verb such as set, another
	// client, Runtime.Changed), the open editor loads the record again
	// (forms.outside-write: reload keeps it open, close leaves it). ""
	// watches nothing; Reload does the same on the application's call.
	Watch string
}
Source file

Registers type
Registers is the component that owns the shared yanks, named registers,
yank history, the register selected for the next operation and the macro
state. It is one instance under app, reached through the register, macro
and yank-history operations and Runtime.Registers(), never through
Runtime fields. Its key rows (q, @, " and the pending modes they enter)
sit on the app noun so a page or list focus can record and replay too.

type Registers struct {
	Named   map[string]Register
	History []Yank
	Active  string
	Macro   MacroState
	// Copy puts a yank's text on the system clipboard (the clipboard
	// register + and the clipboard setting); nil sends it through the
	// terminal as OSC 52 (tea.SetClipboard), which TUIOS and ssh pass on.
	// An app with a clipboard program of its own (pbcopy) sets it.
	Copy func(text string) tea.Cmd
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}

ReplaceOpen makes an open of a kind that is already the top modal close

Request type
Request names an operation and, optionally, the instance it addresses.
Confirmed skips the operation's Confirm prompt: the confirm modal's y sets
it on the stored request, and a script may set it to run unattended.

type Request struct {

	// Actor is an explicit API identity; nil inherits the enclosing request or owner.
	Actor *Actor `json:"actor,omitempty"`

	Verb      string    `json:"verb"`
	Noun      string    `json:"noun"`
	Target    string    `json:"target,omitempty"`
	Args      Arguments `json:"args,omitempty"`
	Confirmed bool      `json:"confirmed,omitempty"`
	// Via is the alias the request was spelled by (an old verb name, a
	// user's actions.json alias): the request is rewritten to the action it
	// names (Noun, Verb) and Via keeps the spelling, so the handler is told
	// which name ran it (Ctx.Invoked) and history shows it. "" for a
	// request that named its action directly.
	Via string `json:"via,omitempty"`
	// Subjects are the instances a request acts on when it acts on
	// several (the rows of a visual selection), in display order; Target
	// is one of them, the one a confirm or a chooser names. Empty means
	// Target alone. A verb that takes many subjects (Operation.Many) runs
	// once with all of them in Ctx.Subjects; any other runs once per
	// subject (Runtime.eachSubject).
	Subjects []string `json:"subjects,omitempty"`
	// Prop, Value and Toggle make the request prop-shaped (props.go):
	// {noun, prop} reads the prop of the thing the request acts for,
	// {noun, prop, value} writes it and {noun, prop, toggle} flips a
	// boolean prop. A prop-shaped request names no verb; it runs as the
	// noun's standard get or set (propVerbRequest), and history keeps it
	// as it was sent. Args then holds at most the subject's key.
	Prop   string `json:"prop,omitempty"`
	Value  any    `json:"value,omitempty"`
	Toggle bool   `json:"toggle,omitempty"`
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}

Response type
Response is the reply to a socket call: a result or an error message.

type Response struct {
	Refused *RefusedError `json:"refused,omitempty"`
	// Result is the operation's result. From HandleCall it is detached
	// from application state: Lines stays Lines (each line encoded), any
	// other value is its JSON encoding as a json.RawMessage, so object
	// keys keep the order the handler's value marshals in (a struct's
	// field order); a nil or null result is nil.
	Result any    `json:"result,omitempty"`
	Error  string `json:"error,omitempty"`
	// Argument is true when Error is an ArgumentError: the request was
	// refused for its arguments, before any instance was chosen.
	Argument bool `json:"argument,omitempty"`
	// Messages are what the call told the user while it ran (ctx.Notify,
	// ctx.ClearNotice, ctx.Status), in order; the runtime's sink got them
	// too. Messages posted later, by a job's Then, reach only the sink.
	Messages []UIMessage `json:"messages,omitempty"`
}

Result.Replacing function / method
Replacing puts the pending work in a slot on its owner: starting it
cancels the owner's running job in the same slot, and a newer job in the
slot cancels this one. Use it for replaceable work such as a preview or a
search, never for independent work.

Result.ReplacingAt function / method
ReplacingAt is Replacing with the slot scoped to scope, the owner or one
of its ancestors: the newest job in (scope, slot) cancels the older one
whichever descendant of scope asked, so previews launched from the rows
of one list replace each other while another list keeps its own. The job
still belongs to the row that asked (its completion and Then land there
and closing it cancels the job); Replacing(slot) is ReplacingAt(owner,
slot). A scope off the owner's ancestry fails the launch.

Runtime type
Runtime is owned by one event loop. Transport callers submit messages to
that loop. It holds the operation registry (Operations, Modes and their
guards), the instance registry (Nouns, Instances and their parent links,
including list rows), the three plain interaction fields Focus, Mode and
Page with the chain of open modals, the pending key-sequence state (Pending, prefix, BindingTimeout) and the hook
that hands off-loop work to the adapter (commands). Components such as
inputs, lists, the picker and the registers are instances, never fields;
TestRuntimeHoldsNoComponentFields pins this list.

type Runtime struct {
	Nouns      map[string]*Noun
	Instances  map[string]*Instance
	Focus      string    // the focused instance; Context() is derived from it
	Mode       ModeState // the one interaction mode; mode.set writes it, a focus change to another stop resets it
	Page       string    // the current page instance; always an ancestor of BaseFocus()
	Modes      map[string]bool
	Operations map[string]Operation
	Quit       bool
	Pending    string
	// BindingTimeout, when positive, is how long every pending key
	// sequence waits on this runtime in place of the sequence-timeout
	// setting (a host or test that fixes it in code); 0, the default,
	// reads the setting.
	BindingTimeout time.Duration
	// ResolveTarget, when set, maps a request target that is not an
	// instance (a foreign id: an extension's row id, a database key) to
	// the instance that stands for it; ExecuteAt consults it (TargetInstance)
	// before refusing an unknown target, so keys, the palette, the CLI and
	// the API all accept the foreign id. An error refuses the request with
	// it; "" and no error leaves the target unknown.
	ResolveTarget func(target string) (string, error)
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}

Runtime.AddVerbAvailability function / method

no-op.

Runtime.Available function / method
Available reports whether an action can run now: its availability (the
verb guard, NounBuilder.When or SetAvailability) holds at the focus in
the mode for the request's arguments, or it has none. The guard reads a
KeyEvent whose Focus and Level are focus, Mode is mode and Args are the
request's (a binding row's own arguments, or a CLI call's), so an action
that acts on an argument (timeline.open {card}) judges that thing. The
resolver runs, at each level, the first row on a key whose action is
available (an unavailable one stands aside and the key goes on, out to
the next level when none at this one is); the palette, the hints, the
help and Execute ask the same question. A guard name that is not
registered hides nothing. A standard verb also reads its props' guards
(PropSpec.When, propsAvailable), and a prop-shaped request is asked as
the get or set it runs as.

func (r *Runtime) Available(q Request, focus string, mode ModeState) bool
Source file

func (r *Runtime) BeforeQuit(name string, check func(r *Runtime, c Context) Verdict) error
Source file
Runtime.Bind function / method
Bind installs rows from config or code onto their noun or instance tables
by identity (mode, keys or pattern, and When): the row with the same
identity is replaced in place, a new row is added (a guarded one before
the unguarded row on its keys, so it answers first while its guard
holds), and an unbind row removes only the row with its identity.
A key sequence in progress (d of d d) is kept unless a row added or
removed is on it: in its mode, with the keys typed so far, a sequence
extending them or one they extend. Such a row cancels the sequence, so
the next key starts fresh against the new tables.

The rows of one call are one batch (rowBatch): a key repeated with the
same identity is not a replacement but an alternative after the first,
so Bind(Do("timeline.open"), Do("timeline.enter")) on l keeps both, and
the first whose action is available answers.

func (r *Runtime) Bind(rows ...Binding) error
Source file

BindActor fixes a transport's identity for all requests, including nested
requests. Request.Actor is ignored during the scope. In-process callers may
use WithActor or Request.Actor when no transport is bound. live distinguishes
a UI loop from a headless shell; this is not an authentication boundary.

func (r *Runtime) BindActor(actor Actor, live bool) (restore func())

Runtime.Builtin function / method
Builtin runs register and marks every operation it registered as the
framework's own (Operation.Builtin). NewRuntime and NewModel wrap their
registrations in it, and so does every framework constructor that
registers operations after them (NewPicker, NewPalette, NewAppearance,
grid.Register, markdown.Register, cli.AddCommands), so an operation
registered lazily is marked too. Operations registered before it, or by
the application at any other time, keep Builtin false. Calls nest. It is
for framework packages; an application's own verbs are not builtin.
Builtin also records each new operation's effective history kind after
the registration batch, including its builder's Rows/State annotations.

func (r *Runtime) Builtin(register func())

Runtime.CanonicalKey function / method
CanonicalKey is an operation key with its noun resolved (CanonicalNoun):
settings.set is ochazuke.settings.set unless the application has a settings
noun of its own. A name is looked up whole first: a registered action or
action alias keeps its name, and a dotted verb (dirs.base.add) resolves
where it was declared; otherwise each dot, last first, is tried as the
noun/verb split, and an unknown name reads its last dot (SplitKey).

Runtime.CanonicalNoun function / method
CanonicalNoun is the registered noun a noun name stands for. A noun the
application registered under that exact name owns it (an app's own
settings noun shadows the framework's alias); otherwise an alias
(settings for ochazuke.settings) names its noun; otherwise the name is
returned unchanged. While framework code registers (Runtime.Builtin) an
alias wins over an application noun of the same name, so the
framework's rows and tables always reach its own nouns. The v0.16.0
spelling app.<noun> of a framework noun is read as ochazuke.<noun>
(deprecated, for one release) unless a noun of that exact name exists or
the app noun has a verb of that name (app.state, an application verb,
shadows the old spelling of ochazuke.state).

func (r *Runtime) CanonicalNoun(name string) string
Source file

Runtime.CheckDefaultBindings function / method
CheckDefaultBindings reports what is wrong with a default bindings file
on this runtime, every problem at once: a file that does not parse, a
table whose noun, instance or mode is unknown, an extends to an unknown
mode, a row that does not validate (an unknown operation, argument,
source or guard), a mode.enter into a mode that is not pending, and two
rows that clash (the same key and guard in one table: the second would
replace the first). It is the rule check for embedded defaults: an
application runs it in a test on its built runtime, after its files
loaded, for each file it embeds.

Runtime.Deliver function / method
Deliver routes a message on the loop: a Completion goes to its owner when
its job is running and is dropped otherwise; any other message is offered
to every component until one claims it. A Then callback that removes its
own owner is safe: the job is already settled and the completion is not
offered to the gone component. It reports whether a component consumed
the message and the delivered completion's error, if any.

Runtime.Emit function / method
Emit tells the runtime an event happened: every hook row on it whose
where matches fields runs its verb, in row order, once the request
running now (if any) has finished, so a verb that closes a modal never
has another verb run in the middle of it. Fields are passed to the row's

Runtime.EnsureConfigSettings function / method
EnsureConfigSettings declares the framework settings whose rows depend
on the application, unless the application declared one of the same
name first: theme (the colour scheme; its choices are the built-in
themes and themes.json's, read here and again when the config directory
changed), pages.hidden (its default the pages declared hidden), the
segments' settings and history-limit while the history is persisted.
The fixed framework settings (settings/ochazuke.json) are declared when
the runtime is made, so they need no call. It applies terminal-leader when an
application declared it (EnsureTerminalLeader). Model.Init and the CLI
shell call it, so settings set works in both; an
application may call it earlier (after setting Config.App) to have them
before the Model starts.

func (r *Runtime) EnsureConfigSettings()
Source file
Runtime.EnsureHistory function / method
EnsureHistory persists the action history, once: it reads the history
file (the last history-limit entries, marked Restored, before what this
session already recorded), compacts it when it holds more or has lines
that do not parse (each such file is one notice), and starts the writer
every later entry is appended through. Nothing happens without a state
directory (Config.Disable, a test binary) or with Config.NoHistoryFile.
Model.Init calls it; the CLI shell does not (a one-shot call is not
history worth keeping).

func (r *Runtime) EnsureHistory()
Source file
Runtime.EnsureHooks function / method
EnsureHooks reads the application's hooks.json once; later calls do
nothing (HookRows reads it again when the config directory changed).
Model.Init and the CLI shell call it after the user bindings, so a
problem is a notice at startup. It returns the problems, each also sent to Config.Report.

Issue admits a job for owner and returns its ticket and the context its

carrying the ticket, checked with Accepts and closed with Settle. A slot
name cancels the owner's running job in that slot; "" accumulates. Launch
and Start build on it for work the runtime runs as a tea.Cmd.

Runtime.JumpFocus function / method
JumpFocus moves the focus steps stops (scope "stop") or regions (scope
"region") forward, or backward for negative steps, through the focus
root in drawing order (units), wrapping. The current unit is the nearest stop (or
region) on the focus path; the next one is the first after that unit's
whole subtree. A stop is never descended into (a grid is one stop, its
cells are not); the focus lands on the unit's remembered child.

func (r *Runtime) JumpFocus(steps int, scope string) error

Runtime.LoadBindings function / method
LoadBindings installs rows from a JSON array in the binding row shape and

func (r *Runtime) LoadBindings(data []byte) ([]Binding, error)
Source file
Runtime.LoadDefaultBindings function / method
LoadDefaultBindings installs a default bindings file (the grouped
format, ParseBindings in bindings_file.go) into the default
layer: the noun and instance tables, below the user's bindings.json,
exactly where the same rows bound in Go would sit. Modes come first,
then each group's rows in file order (a row replaces the row with its
identity in place, a guarded row goes before the unguarded row on its
keys, a sequence gets its prefix cancel), then the group's extends.

Unlike a user file, an embedded default is checked strictly: an unknown
operation, argument, source, guard, mode or instance is an error naming
source, the table and the key, and the rows after it are not installed.
An application loads its files at setup, after it registered the verbs
and guards they name and before Model.Init applies the user's file;
MustLoadDefaultBindings panics instead, so a test that builds the app
catches a bad row. source tags the rows in bindings defaults ("mtm/
bindings/app.json").

func (r *Runtime) LoadDefaultBindings(source string, data []byte) error
Source file
Runtime.LoadDefaultBindingsFS function / method
LoadDefaultBindingsFS installs every file of fsys matching the patterns
(fs.Glob), each pattern's files in name order, each tagged with its path.
A pattern that matches nothing is an error.

func (r *Runtime) LoadDefaultBindingsFS(fsys fs.FS, patterns ...string) error

OnConfigReload adds an application's own configuration to config.reload:

registration order after the framework's files. Each problem not already

Source file
Runtime.OpenArgsForm function / method
OpenArgsForm opens the args form over the focus for a request whose
verb lacks arguments: one field per parameter (ArgsFields), prefilled
from q's arguments, the first missing required one focused (typing,
when it is a text field). Every field is filled by the user: nothing is
read from the focus or a guess (R12.5), except what a parameter declares
and the form shows: a Focus parameter starts holding the focused thing
(focusValue), and an Edits one the subject's current value of its prop,
selected as a rename is, or empty with the prop's Fallback as its
placeholder (argsFormStart). enter runs the form once every
required field is valid (form.run) and otherwise goes to the field that
still needs a value; esc in normal mode cancels and runs nothing.
run executes the completed request after the form closed, at the focus
it opened over; its error reopens the form with the values. When the
only missing required parameter is a path and SinglePathSetting allows,
no form opens: the path chooser does (the editor returned is nil), and
its pick runs the request.

func (r *Runtime) OpenArgsForm(q Request, run func(Request) error) (*RecordEditor, error)

Runtime.PinInstance function / method
PinInstance makes id declare mode as its base mode (Instance.Pin), "" to
drop the pin. The focus landing on id, or entering a stop beneath it
with no nearer pin, puts the runtime in mode; mode.set {mode: declared}
returns to it. A re-pin applies at once: when the focus beneath any
modal declares through id and the runtime stands in the mode it
declared before (not in a mode the user lifted into, such as insert),
the base mode follows in the same update, so the next frame's badge
shows it; under an open modal the mode the outermost modal restores on
close follows instead. A pin names a registered base mode.

func (r *Runtime) PinInstance(id, mode string) error
Source file
Runtime.Placed function / method
Placed records that the instance id is being drawn now, during its page's
render: the order of these calls in a frame is the page's drawing order,
which focus jumps (focus.next, focus.previous) and list spills walk, so
motions follow the screen as drawn and cannot drift from it. List, Input
(InputBlock), Terminal and grid views call it; a component of the
application's own calls it from its view. Outside a page render it does
nothing. There is no other way to order stops: before the first frame the
order is the tree's, and once a frame drew any stop, a stop the last
frame did not draw is skipped (it is not on screen). A stop drawn by an
application view that never calls Placed is therefore skipped too.

func (r *Runtime) Placed(id string)
Source file
Runtime.PlacedAt function / method
PlacedAt records where the view text of instance id holds the focus
(lines focus to focus+height of text; a list's selected row, an input's
whole box) and returns text marked so its page finds the body line it
lands on, which is how the page scrolls to keep the focus visible. Call
it with the view's final text after Placed; it amends that placement (or
places id, if Placed was not called). Outside a page render it returns
text unchanged. List, Input, Terminal and grid views call it.

func (r *Runtime) PlacedAt(id, text string, focus, height int) string
Source file

Runtime.ReloadConfig function / method
ReloadConfig reads every configuration file the application uses again,
in dependency order: themes.json (when it was read: the theme setting's
choices are then current), settings.json (when settings are declared;
observers see every value that changed, the Model's theme among them),
actions.json (when it was installed), bindings.json (when it was
applied, once: a row can name an action's verb), segments.json (when it
was read), then each
OnConfigReload hook. Each file's last problems are withdrawn and its new
ones reported, so no stale notice survives; a bad file is skipped, never
fatal. It returns the problems in that order, each naming its file, and
an error only when the application has no config directory.

func (r *Runtime) ReloadConfig() ([]string, error)
Source file
Runtime.ReloadHooks function / method
ReloadHooks reads hooks.json again: the problems of the last read are
taken back, a row that does not validate is skipped and reported (an
unknown event or where key is a warning; the row loads), a file that does
not parse loads no rows. A missing file is no rows and no problem.
config.reload reads it too.

func (r *Runtime) ReloadHooks() []string

already loaded user bindings file above them. It validates every row
before changing any table; an error leaves the old rows and user undo log
intact. source must be nonempty and cannot be SourceGo or SourceGenerated.
Modes and table inheritance are unchanged; declare those before calling.
A nil slice removes the source's rows. User rows for removed defaults
remain in bindings.json and are reapplied with a notice (an unbind has
nothing to remove until that default returns). Call on the runtime's loop.

func (r *Runtime) SetDefaultBindings(source string, rows []Binding) error
Source file

Runtime.StopBindings function / method
StopBindings is the app-wide keyboard: the effective rows of every focus
stop, as ListBindings gives them for the focus a jump to the stop lands
on. The order is fixed: the pages in tab order (PageList), each page's
stops in drawing order (the order focus.next walks, undrawn stops after
in tree order), then the open modals bottom to top with theirs, then app
in normal mode. The mode is the one the focus would have there: the
current mode at the current focus, else the stop's pinned mode (a prompt
is insert, a terminal terminal), else normal. Nothing moves: the focus
and mode are back as they were on return.

It differs from bindings.list --all, which exports every stored row
(each noun's and instance's tables, every mode, as configuration JSON),
active or not: this answers "what does each key do where", after
shadowing, guards and the modal boundary.

func (r *Runtime) StopBindings() []StopBindings
Source file
Runtime.StoreYank function / method
StoreYank puts a yank into the shared registers with the bookkeeping the
editor's yank operations use: the named or active register, the unnamed
register, "0" for a yank or "1".."9" and "-" for a deletion, and the yank
history. A component outside the package (a grid) yanks shapes the editor
does not know ("column"), which register.set refuses, so it stores through
this call and the editor still sees the text.

func (r *Runtime) StoreYank(y Yank, register string, deletion bool) error

Runtime.TypingShadows function / method
TypingShadows reports every printable key that a place taking text does
not insert: for each instance as the focus and each base mode, where a
level of the walk types (a row on the printable class, available, its
guard holding), every printable character and space must reach that
row's action. A row on a printable key that answers first (a capture
row, an inner level's row, a mode-less row of a noun nearer the focus)
or a sequence starting with one is reported: while a field takes text,
only keys a person cannot type (ctrl, alt, esc, enter, tab, the arrows)
may be bound. While a modal is open only the instances inside it are
checked: the live focus is always there. Instances with no rows of
their own under the same parents and of the same noun (a list's rows)
are checked once. The check reads the runtime's present state (open modals,
filters, guards) and runs nothing; the focus and mode are restored.

func (r *Runtime) TypingShadows() []string
Source file

SavedFilter type
SavedFilter is a named AND of conditions. Where is the convenient
equality form, matching --where; Conditions supplies other operators.
List scopes a row to one list/tree id or chooser kind, Noun to every row view of those rows.
A list-specific row wins over a noun row with the same name.

type SavedFilter struct {
	Name       string         `json:"name"`
	List       string         `json:"list,omitempty"`
	Noun       string         `json:"noun,omitempty"`
	Where      map[string]any `json:"where,omitempty"`
	Conditions []PropFilter   `json:"conditions,omitempty"`
}
Source file
Scheme type
Scheme is a named colour scheme: the palette roles NewStyles draws
pages, panels, inputs and modals with. Background is which side of each
role it draws: "adaptive" (the terminal's, the default), "light" or
"dark". Text is body text and the title; Muted hints and inactive tabs;
Accent the active tab, badge, subtitle and focused borders; Border
unfocused panel borders; Surface the active tab's and badge's
background; Error errors, kept apart from Accent and Muted. Selection
stays reverse video in every scheme, so the selected row reads without
colour.

type Scheme struct {
	Background string               `json:"background,omitempty"`
	Text       SchemeColor          `json:"text"`
	Muted      SchemeColor          `json:"muted"`
	Accent     SchemeColor          `json:"accent"`
	Border     SchemeColor          `json:"border"`
	Surface    SchemeColor          `json:"surface"`
	Error      SchemeColor          `json:"error"`
	Tones      map[string]ToneStyle `json:"tones,omitempty"`
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}
Source file
SchemeColor type
SchemeColor is one palette role: Light is drawn on a light background,
Dark on a dark one. Each is "#RRGGBB", "#RGB" or an ANSI index "0" to
"255". In themes.json a role is one colour for both, or
{"light": "...", "dark": "..."}.

type SchemeColor struct {
	Light string `json:"light"`
	Dark  string `json:"dark"`
}

SearchCaseSmart, SearchCaseIgnore, SearchCaseSensitive constant
The values of search.case.

const (
	// SearchCaseSmart ignores case unless the query holds an upper-case
	// letter, then matches case exactly (Vim's ignorecase with smartcase).
	SearchCaseSmart = "smart"
	// SearchCaseIgnore always ignores case (Vim's ignorecase).
	SearchCaseIgnore = "ignore"
	// SearchCaseSensitive always matches case (Vim's noignorecase).
	SearchCaseSensitive = "sensitive"
)

Source file
Segment type
Segment is one status segment row. Format is a template over {{label}}
and {{value}} (default "{{label}} {{value}}", or "{{value}}" without a
label). Exactly one of Provider, Command and Verb is the source; Args
are the verb's or the provider's arguments, and a string argument may
name a setting as "{name}", read on the loop at each refresh. Every is the refresh interval (default
DefaultSegmentEvery, at least MinSegmentEvery) and Timeout the most one
refresh may take (default DefaultSegmentTimeout, at most Every). Tone is
a named tone (tone.go) the text draws in, unless the answer brings its
own (SegmentValue.Tone); none draws muted like the rule. Order sorts
the rows (lower first, then the order they were added) before
status.segment-order. Enabled is the default of the segment's setting
(nil: on). Path is the provider's path (disk free reads it).

In segments.json the durations are strings ("2s"), as settings.json
writes them.

type Segment struct {
	ID       string        `json:"id"`
	Label    string        `json:"label,omitempty"`
	Format   string        `json:"format,omitempty"`
	Provider string        `json:"provider,omitempty"`
	Command  Argv          `json:"command,omitempty"`
	Verb     string        `json:"verb,omitempty"`
	Args     Arguments     `json:"args,omitempty"`
	Every    time.Duration `json:"-"`
	Timeout  time.Duration `json:"-"`
	Tone     string        `json:"tone,omitempty"`
	Order    int           `json:"order,omitempty"`
	Enabled  *bool         `json:"enabled,omitempty"`
	Path     string        `json:"path,omitempty"`
	// Description is the line segment list and the segment's setting show.
	Description string `json:"description,omitempty"`
	// Owner is the settings owner of the segment's setting (a tab of the
	// Settings page, Setting.Owner): an extension's segment names the
	// extension's owner; "" is the declaring application's.
	Owner string `json:"owner,omitempty"`
}
Source file

SelectionChar, SelectionLine, SelectionBlock constant
Selection shapes: a mode that holds a selection says which shape it
selects (DefineSelection, a bindings file's "modes" entry
{"selection": "line"}). Code asks the shape, never a mode's name: the
editor draws and edits by it, a list starts its row selection when a
line-shaped mode is entered on it (list_visual.go).

const (
	SelectionChar  = "char"  // characters from the anchor to the cursor (visual)
	SelectionLine  = "line"  // whole lines or list rows (visual-line, V)
	SelectionBlock = "block" // a rectangle (visual-block)
)
Source file
SequenceTimeoutSetting, SequenceUnknownSetting, SequencePass, SequenceSwallow, SequenceInheritedSetting, SequenceDrop, SequenceKeep, RenameSelectSetting, CreateFromFocusSetting, DeleteArchiveSetting, DeleteArchiveConfirmSetting, CLIStandardVerbsSetting, CLIStandardVerbsLine, CLIStandardVerbsList, VisualAfterActionSetting, VisualAfterEnd, VisualAfterKeep, VisualListSetting, VisualListMode, VisualListMarks, RepeatEmptySetting, RepeatEmptyInfo, RepeatEmptySilent constant
The framework settings the key resolver reads.

const (
	// SequenceTimeoutSetting is how long every pending key sequence (a
	// multi-key row's prefix such as g of g g, and a row entering a
	// pending mode with a prefix, such as the terminal leader) waits for
	// its next key before the shorter binding runs: a duration, 0 (the
	// default) waits until a key comes.
	SequenceTimeoutSetting = "sequence-timeout"
	// SequenceUnknownSetting says what a key no row continues a pending
	// sequence with does: SequencePass (the default) or SequenceSwallow.
	SequenceUnknownSetting = "sequence-unknown"
	// SequencePass ends the sequence, runs its shorter binding (the
	// leader alone: send it to the pane) and handles the key as if it
	// were typed alone.
	SequencePass = "pass"
	// SequenceSwallow drops the sequence and the key, with a notice
	// naming the keys.
	SequenceSwallow = "swallow"
	// SequenceInheritedSetting says whether a sequence a mode inherits
	// from the mode it extends (a row noun's normal d d, read in
	// visual-line) waits over a row the mode binds on its own for the
	// keys typed (the list's visual-line d): SequenceDrop (the default)
	// lets the mode's own row answer at once, SequenceKeep lets the
	// inherited sequence wait, as before.
	SequenceInheritedSetting = "sequence-inherit"
	// SequenceDrop: an inherited sequence never shadows the mode's own
	// shorter row.
	SequenceDrop = "drop"
	// SequenceKeep: an inherited sequence waits as any other does.
	SequenceKeep = "keep"
	// RenameSelectSetting opens a rename (outline.edit without select, a
	// RecordSpec with Rename) with the whole text selected (Input.SelectAll):
	// typing replaces it, a motion keeps it. Default on; off opens it with
	// the cursor at the end.
	RenameSelectSetting = "rename.select"
	// CreateFromFocusSetting lets the standard create fill a reference
	// prop declared FromFocus from the focused thing of its noun when the
	// request omits it, the fill shown in the palette slot, the args form
	// and create's notice. Default on; off fills nothing and the reference
	// stays empty (never a refusal).
	CreateFromFocusSetting = "create.from-focus"
	// DeleteArchiveSetting makes the standard delete archive a live thing
	// of a noun with a RoleArchive prop (it sets the prop) and delete an
	// archived one for real; nouns without the role are unchanged. Default
	// off: delete is the store's delete.
	DeleteArchiveSetting = "delete.archive"
	// DeleteArchiveConfirmSetting is whether a delete that will only
	// archive (DeleteArchiveSetting) asks first, with DefaultArchiveConfirm.
	// Default on; off archives without asking (archive is undone by
	// restore). The real delete of an archived thing always asks.
	DeleteArchiveConfirmSetting = "delete.archive-confirm"
	// CLIStandardVerbsSetting is how the CLI's help shows a noun's
	// standard verbs (cli.Mount): CLIStandardVerbsLine (the default,
	// owner decision 24) names them on one line of the noun's help and
	// after "· std" in its summary, its named verbs keeping their
	// entries; CLIStandardVerbsList lists each as its own entry, as
	// before standard verbs. Every verb runs either way.
	CLIStandardVerbsSetting = "cli.standard-verbs"
	CLIStandardVerbsLine    = "line"
	CLIStandardVerbsList    = "list"
	// VisualAfterActionSetting is what a key acting on a visual selection
	// of list rows leaves: VisualAfterEnd (the default, as in Vim) or
	// VisualAfterKeep.
	VisualAfterActionSetting = "visual.after-action"
	// VisualAfterEnd ends the selection and leaves visual-line.
	VisualAfterEnd = "end"
	// VisualAfterKeep keeps the selection over the rows still shown.
	VisualAfterKeep = "keep"
	// VisualListSetting is what V does on a list: VisualListMode (the
	// default) enters visual-line; VisualListMarks selects in normal
	// mode, the older selection.
	VisualListSetting = "visual.list"
	// VisualListMode enters visual-line.
	VisualListMode = "mode"
	// VisualListMarks selects in normal mode.
	VisualListMarks = "marks"
	// RepeatEmptySetting is what dot-repeat with nothing to repeat at the
	// focus does: RepeatEmptyInfo (the default) or RepeatEmptySilent.
	RepeatEmptySetting = "repeat.empty"
	// RepeatEmptyInfo says "nothing to repeat" on the status line, plain.
	RepeatEmptyInfo = "info"
	// RepeatEmptySilent shows nothing.
	RepeatEmptySilent = "silent"
)

Setting type
Setting is one declared user preference: its name, type, default, the
line the settings list shows and, for a string, the allowed values.
Settings are not for secrets: the file is plain JSON in the config
directory.

type Setting struct {
	Name        string   `json:"name"`
	Type        string   `json:"type"`
	Default     any      `json:"default"`
	Description string   `json:"description,omitempty"`
	Enum        []string `json:"enum,omitempty"`
	// Owner says which owner (a tab of the Settings page) the setting
	// belongs to: "" is the declaring application (OwnerApp), unless the
	// framework declares it (its embedded rows, a feature's declaration
	// inside Runtime.Builtin: OwnerFramework). An application that
	// declares a framework row's name to change its default keeps the
	// framework's owner unless it sets Owner itself. Settings.OwnerOf
	// resolves it.
	Owner string `json:"owner,omitempty"`
	// Section files the setting under a section of its owner; "" takes
	// the name before its first dot ("tree.zoom.root" is in "tree"), and
	// a name without a dot is in SettingsGeneralSection (SectionName).
	// DeclareSettingSection gives a section its title, order and line.
	Section string `json:"section,omitempty"`
	// Group, when set, puts the setting under a heading row of that name
	// inside its section (the schedules of an application, each with its
	// own settings).
	Group string `json:"group,omitempty"`
	// Validate, when set, checks a value of the right type further: Set
	// refuses a value it rejects and a file value it rejects reads the
	// default, as a value of the wrong type does.
	Validate func(value any) error `json:"-"`
	// Deprecated are older names of the setting, kept for one release:
	// settings.get, set and reset, Value and settings.json read them as
	// this setting, each warning once, and a write moves the file's value
	// to this name. A declaration under an old name declares this one.
	Deprecated []string `json:"deprecated,omitempty"`
}
Source file

("tree.zoom.root" is in "tree"), else SettingsGeneralSection.

SettingOwner type
SettingOwner declares an owner of settings: a tab of the Settings page.
Owners are drawn by Order (lowest first), then in the order they were
declared, then the owners nobody declared in the order of their first
setting. The framework declares the application (OwnerApp, order 0) and
itself (OwnerFramework, order 100), so an application's owners (one
for its extensions) fall between them unless they give an Order.

type SettingOwner struct {
	ID          string `json:"id"`
	Title       string `json:"title,omitempty"`
	Description string `json:"description,omitempty"`
	Order       int    `json:"order,omitempty"`
}

Settings type
Settings holds the declared settings and their values from settings.json
in the config directory. It is part of the config component, reached
through Runtime.Settings(). The file is read on first use (and again
when the config directory changes), written atomically (a temp file
renamed over it) on every Set and Reset, and keeps every key it holds:
an unknown or invalid key is reported and written back unchanged, never
dropped. Without a config directory the values last for the session.
Model polls the file (SettingsPoll) so a value another process
set (the CLI) reaches a running TUI.

type Settings struct {
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}

Settings.ResetAll function / method
ResetAll sets every setting back to its default by the writes-through
rule: with a session (and autosave off) each setting not at its default
gets the default as a session value (Write removes the file's keys,
RevertAll takes it back); otherwise every declared setting's key is
removed from settings.json in one write (unknown keys stay) and the
session values are dropped. It returns the layer written and the names
it reset.

func (s *Settings) ResetAll() (string, []string, error)

SettingsLayoutSetting, SettingsLayoutTabsSections, SettingsLayoutTabs, SettingsLayoutSections, SettingsNarrowWidthSetting, SettingsSearchValuesSetting, SettingsAllSectionSetting constant
The Settings page's layout (settings.layout): which owners and sections
(settings_owners.go) are tabs across the top and which are rows of the
section list on the left.

  - tabs+sections (the default): a tab per owner (drawn with more than
    one), the shown tab's sections in the section list, the shown
    section's settings over the detail on the right. Below
    settings.narrow-width columns the section list folds into a selector
    line at the top of the settings panel.
  - tabs: a tab per section, every owner's, and no section list (the
    page before owners).
  - sections: no tabs; the section list holds every owner's sections,
    each owner's under a heading row (Vim's :options contents).

The section list (settingsSectionsID) is an ordinary List whose rows are
the ochazuke.settings-section noun; it is drawn before the settings
list, so j past its last row spills into the settings (R3.4). A section
row's verbs (open, reset, write, revert, copy, pick) have twins at owner
level on ochazuke.settings-tab, sharing the handlers. ] and [
(settings.tab) step the tabs; tab and shift+tab (settings.section
{across}) walk every section in drawing order.

const (
	// SettingsLayoutSetting is the Settings page's layout:
	// SettingsLayoutTabsSections (the default), SettingsLayoutTabs or
	// SettingsLayoutSections.
	SettingsLayoutSetting      = "settings.layout"
	SettingsLayoutTabsSections = "tabs+sections"
	SettingsLayoutTabs         = "tabs"
	SettingsLayoutSections     = "sections"
	// SettingsNarrowWidthSetting is the page width below which the section
	// list folds into a selector line; 0 never folds.
	SettingsNarrowWidthSetting = "settings.narrow-width"
	// SettingsSearchValuesSetting makes the page's / filters search every
	// setting: a section row matches by its settings' names, descriptions,
	// values, defaults and choices, the section list's filter spans every
	// tab, and the settings pane shows the shown section's matches. Off,
	// a section row matches by its title alone (the page before it).
	SettingsSearchValuesSetting = "settings.search-values"
	// SettingsAllSectionSetting lists an All section first in the section
	// list (and an All tab with settings.layout tabs): every setting in one
	// list, each under its tab and section. A / search in the section list
	// (with settings.search-values) lands on it, so every match shows at
	// once. Off, there is no All section and a search shows the shown
	// section's matches (the page before it).
	SettingsAllSectionSetting = "settings.all-section"
)
Source file
SettingsPageID, SettingsGeneral constant
The Settings page: settings.edit opens (or switches to) a framework page
of the declared settings, laid out by settings.layout
(settings_page_layout.go): by default the owners are tabs across the top
(the application, its declared owners, ochazuke; ] and [ step them), the
shown tab's sections a list on the left (tab and shift+tab walk every
section in drawing order, across the tabs), and the shown section one
list of rows on the right, a setting a row: its full name on the left (a
• after a name whose value is not the default), its value on the right
(‹ value › for one h/l steps); a group's rows sit under its heading. A
list setting's row reads its item count, and each item is a row beneath
it with the name column blank. Under the list the selected setting's
description says what its default is, where its value comes from (the
default, settings.json, or the default because settings.json holds an
invalid value) and what it takes; J/K scroll it.

Every change is saved as it is made; there is no save key. On a setting
row enter edits (a boolean flips, an enum takes its next value, a list
takes a new item, any other type opens a one-line prompt prefilled with
the value), l/h and space step a boolean, an enum or a number, n adds an
item to a list, r (or backspace, delete) resets to the default. On an
item row enter or i edits the item, n adds one after it, d d deletes
it, r resets the whole list. u (or ctrl+z) takes back the last change
made on the page. q or esc (settings.close) goes back to the page the
Settings page was opened from, with its remembered focus and mode; the
page reopens on the section and row it was left on. A value another
process sets (the poll) redraws the open lists.

settings.edit registers the page on first use and settings.close
removes it again, so it is a tab only while it is open; an application
that wants it as a permanent tab calls AddSettingsPage.

const (
	// SettingsPageID is the Settings page's instance id.
	SettingsPageID = "__settings"
	// SettingsGeneral titles the section of the settings without one.
	SettingsGeneral = "General"
)

SourceKey, SourceDigit, SourceText, SourceCount, SourceLine, SourceSteps, SourceOp, SourceOpCount, SourcePrefix, SourceLetter, SourceRow constant
Sources a From entry may name: "key" (the key name), "digit" (the key as an
integer), "text" (pasted or typed text), "count" (payload count, at least 1),
"line" (payload count, 0 when absent, else the operator count), "steps"
(count times operator count), "op" (payload operator), "opcount", "prefix"
(the key sequence the pending mode holds), "letter" (the key's letter,
lowercased, without ctrl+ or shift+: f, F, shift+f and ctrl+f all read
f; "" for a key that is not one letter), "row" (the row's Data, see
FromRow) and "row:<field>" (one field of it). A From value may name
several sources separated by spaces ("prefix key"): their texts are
joined with a space, the way terminal.send reads a key list.

const (
	SourceKey     = "key"
	SourceDigit   = "digit"
	SourceText    = "text"
	SourceCount   = "count"
	SourceLine    = "line"
	SourceSteps   = "steps"
	SourceOp      = "op"
	SourceOpCount = "opcount"
	SourcePrefix  = "prefix"
	SourceLetter  = "letter"
	SourceRow     = "row"
)

SpillEdge type
SpillEdge is one edge a list-like component's motions can spill past
(move the focus to the neighbouring stop): the rows' first and last, and
a table's first and last column. Every component that spills (a list, a
table, a tree's list, a timeline's cards and steps) names its edges with
this one set, so list.no-spill traps each of the four independently and
no component misses a direction.

type SpillEdge string
Source file
SpillEdges variable
SpillEdges is every edge, the suffixes a list.no-spill entry takes
(ID:last, ID:first-col, ...). An entry without one names all four. A
component with no horizontal motion (a plain list, a timeline) accepts
the column edges as a no-op: it never spills sideways.

var SpillEdges = []SpillEdge{EdgeFirst, EdgeLast, EdgeFirstColumn, EdgeLastColumn}

name splits where it was declared (a verb may hold a dot: dirs.base.add),

Source file
StandardVerbsSetting, StandardVerbsLast, StandardVerbsMixed, StandardVerbsHidden, StandardVerbsTag, StandardVerbsRecord constant
The palette.standard-verbs setting (plan 2.10, owner decision 25): one
list setting for every standard-verb change to the palette. Its parts
are an ordering (StandardVerbsLast, StandardVerbsMixed or
StandardVerbsHidden; none is mixed, today's one alphabetical list) and
the flags StandardVerbsTag and StandardVerbsRecord. A part left out
returns its piece to today's behaviour, so [] turns them all off.

const (
	StandardVerbsSetting = "palette.standard-verbs"
	// StandardVerbsLast lists a noun's named verbs before its standard
	// ones in the palette's verb column (the default).
	StandardVerbsLast = "last"
	// StandardVerbsMixed lists them in one alphabetical list.
	StandardVerbsMixed = "mixed"
	// StandardVerbsHidden leaves standard verbs out of the verb column;
	// typing the name, the CLI and bindings still reach them.
	StandardVerbsHidden = "hidden"
	// StandardVerbsTag marks a standard verb "std" in the verb column.
	StandardVerbsTag = "tag"
	// StandardVerbsRecord shows a standard get's answer (one thing) as
	// prop and value rows in the palette's results view.
	StandardVerbsRecord = "record"
)
Source file

StandardizeJSONC function / method
StandardizeJSONC is data as standard JSON: each comment and trailing
comma becomes spaces, so every byte offset (and so every line and
column) is the original file's. Plain JSON is returned as is, without a
copy. A file that is not JSONC is an error naming its line and column.

func StandardizeJSONC(data []byte) ([]byte, error)

Source file
Store type
Store is a noun's row in the handler table: its kind and the handlers
that read and write its things, each optional. A standard verb exists on
the noun exactly when its handler is set (standardVerbs' Needs). Get
answers the thing with that key ("" for a singleton); List answers the
things. ListParams are list's parameters beside where, props and limit.

Create stores a new thing from the props given (typed, each prop's
Default filling an omitted one) and answers it, its key filled. Patch
writes the changed props of the thing with that key and answers the
thing after the write (nil: the runtime reads it back with Get). Delete
removes the thing with that key.

Confirm is a standard verb's confirm prompt by verb ({{label}} is the
thing's label prop, else its Fallback); delete asks
DefaultDeleteConfirm unless the store names its own ("" asks nothing).
When is a standard verb's availability by verb: a guard name, set on
the generated verb as SetAvailability does.

Watch, when set, answers watch itself (standard_watch.go): it pushes
each event to emit, from any goroutine, until stop; without it watch
lists again (List, a singleton's Get) whenever things may have changed.

type Store struct {
	Kind       string
	Get        func(Ctx, string) (Thing, error)
	List       func(Ctx, ListQuery) ([]Thing, error)
	Create     func(Ctx, Thing) (Thing, error)
	Patch      func(Ctx, string, Thing) (Thing, error)
	Delete     func(Ctx, string) error
	ListParams map[string]Parameter
	Watch      func(c Ctx, q WatchQuery, emit func(WatchEvent)) (stop func(), err error)
	Confirm    map[string]string
	// ConfirmFor builds the prompt a standard verb that asks (delete, or
	// one Confirm gives a prompt) asks for one request: it gets the
	// request's context and arguments, the verb, the keys of the things
	// it acts on (one, or a visual selection's) and the prompt it would
	// ask (Confirm's, the default, or the archive prompt), and answers
	// the template to ask ({{noun}}, {{label}} and the rest still
	// expand); "" asks nothing. It is how a store says what a delete
	// costs (a task's subtasks and notes left without it) without
	// replacing the standard verb. nil asks the prompt as it is.
	ConfirmFor func(c Ctx, verb string, keys []string, prompt string) string
	When       map[string]string
	// Undo lets history.undo revert this store's standard changes
	// (standard_undo.go); AcceptsKey says Create keeps a key the values
	// give, so a delete can be undone under its old key.
	Undo       bool
	AcceptsKey bool
	// Live, on an instances store, answers the ids of the live instances
	// that are its things, in order; nil is every instance of the noun in
	// registration order. (Pages are ochazuke.page's things whatever noun
	// their instances have.)
	Live func(r *Runtime) []string
}

Styles.CardBorders function / method
CardBorders are the border styles of a card timeline's three states
(the selection is the border, never reverse video): selected while the
timeline has the focus, the accent in bold; selected without the focus,
halfway from the accent to the border role; every other card, the
border role faded toward the surface by dim-unfocused. With no colour
(mono, NO_COLOR) they are bold, plain and faint; the timeline also
draws the two selected states in heavy lines, so they read without
any attribute.

func (s Styles) CardBorders() (focused, selected, other lipgloss.Style)
