TableState type
TableState is what a table remembers across a restart: its current
column (a cell-mode table's), the widths its columns were given with
z > and z < (column index to cells; a column left out is automatic) and
the pinned columns the user chose (any table's; nil keeps the default
pins, an empty list pins none).

Version is the format the widths were written in: tableStateVersion
(1) since a row-select table remembers the widths z F gives it; a
row-select table ignores widths without it (left by v0.12.0, when every
table was cell-based).

type TableState struct {
	Column  int         `json:"column,omitempty"`
	Widths  map[int]int `json:"widths,omitempty"`
	Pins    *[]int      `json:"pins,omitempty"`
	Version int         `json:"version,omitempty"`
}
Source file
TableText function / method
TableText lays a table out as plain text lines, the header line first,
with the layout a list with columns draws (List.View) and no styling,
gutter or highlight: a printer outside the TUI (the command line's
--format table) reads the same column declarations. At width > 0 the
columns that do not fit drop by Priority and cells cut with "…" at their
width; at width <= 0 nothing drops or cuts, every column is as wide as
its widest cell or title (Width and Min are ignored). A row with fewer
cells than columns has empty ones; control characters in a cell become
spaces; trailing spaces are trimmed. No columns is no lines.

func TableText(cols []Column, rows [][]string, width int) []string
Source file

TablesFile is the remembered table views in the state directory: an
object of table id (a list's, a grid's) to TableState.

const TablesFile = "tables.json"

Terminal type
Terminal is the component of a terminal instance: it holds the last frame
the stream delivered, renders it into the area a page gives it and reports
the pane's cursor. Frames arrive through the delivery contract as
Completions owned by the instance in the "read" slot, one read at a time,
so a frame that arrives after Close, or after a newer read started, is
stale and dropped before it reaches the component.

type Terminal struct {
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}

Terminal.SetOverlay function / method
SetOverlay draws o over the pane from the next frame on; nil clears it.
The overlay stays until the application clears or replaces it: a new
frame from the stream does not clear it, and neither do a focus change,
a mode change or a detach, so an application that pairs it with a mode
clears it where it leaves the mode (and in its own focus or close
handling). It changes only what ViewBlock draws: Frame, the stream and
every other reader of the pane see the pane's own cells.

func (t *Terminal) SetOverlay(o *TerminalOverlay)

Source file
TerminalFrame type
TerminalFrame is one rendering of a pane: its rows of trusted ANSI, the
cursor cell, the cursor shape the pane reports ("block", "bar",
"underline"; "" for the default block) and whether the cursor is hidden.
Status is the stream's own state beside its cells ("running", "exited 0")
for streams whose application draws that state; the framework does not
draw it. A read whose cells are unchanged is not redrawn unless its
Status changed, so a stream reports here any change an application shows
outside the cells (an exit, a reconnect), even while the pane is hidden.

type TerminalFrame struct {
	Lines  []string `json:"lines"`
	Cursor Pos      `json:"cursor"`
	Shape  string   `json:"shape,omitempty"`
	Hidden bool     `json:"hidden,omitempty"`
	Status string   `json:"status,omitempty"`
}

TerminalOverlay type
TerminalOverlay is what an application draws over a terminal's frame
without touching the frame: Dim fades the pane's cells (the modal-dim
backdrop style; faint in mono and NO_COLOR) and each mark replaces the
cells it covers. Hints over a live pane (labels on its URLs and paths)
are an overlay plus an application mode; see SetOverlay.

type TerminalOverlay struct {
	Dim   bool          `json:"dim,omitempty"`
	Marks []OverlayMark `json:"marks,omitempty"`
}
Source file
TerminalReleaser type
TerminalReleaser is a TerminalStream that holds something to give back
when its terminal closes: a daemon size lease, a connection. Terminal.Close
calls Release once, so Pop, SetItems, RemoveInstance and a CloseOnDetach
detach all release the stream; its error is kept as the terminal's Err.

type TerminalReleaser interface {
	Release() error
}
Source file
TerminalStream type
TerminalStream is the application's connection to one pane: a daemon
client, never a PTY the framework owns. The framework reads frames from
it, forwards keys and pasted text to it and tells it the pane's size.
Next blocks until the pane changes and returns its next frame; it returns
ctx's error when the terminal instance closes or newer work supersedes
the read. Keys are named as bindings spell them ("ctrl+a", "d", "esc").

type TerminalStream interface {
	Next(ctx context.Context) (TerminalFrame, error)
	Send(keys ...string) error
	Paste(text string) error
	Resize(cols, rows int) error
}

Source file
TextFormatPlain, TextFormatMarkdown, TextFormatPreformatted constant
Text formats: how a text shown in a modal is written, and so how it is
drawn. A verb declares its answer's format through Operation.Result
(NounBuilder.TextResult, MarkdownResult, PreformattedResult); a caller
names it in TextDoc.Format. The CLI and the API return the text as it
is, whatever its format.

const (
	// TextFormatPlain is text to read, select and yank as written: the
	// text view (OpenTextView), a read-only editor.
	TextFormatPlain = ResultText
	// TextFormatMarkdown is Markdown: headings, lists, code fences, links.
	TextFormatMarkdown = ResultMarkdown
	// TextFormatPreformatted is text laid out for a terminal (a --help
	// page): drawn as it is written, never read as Markdown.
	TextFormatPreformatted = ResultPreformatted
)

Source file
Ticket type
Ticket names one job: the instance that owns it, its runtime-wide id and
the slot it was started in. Slot is "" for a job that accumulates (every
completion is delivered, in finish order) and a name for a job that
replaces: a newer job in the same slot on the same owner (or, with
ReplacingAt, on the same scope) drops the older.
A completion is stale, and dropped, only when its job was cancelled by a
newer job in its slot or by its owner closing.

type Ticket struct {
	Owner string `json:"owner"`
	Job   uint64 `json:"job"`
	Slot  string `json:"slot,omitempty"`
}
Source file
Timeline type
Timeline is a scrolling column of cards of varying height, each a box
(Card). The selection, motions, counts, spills, the / filter and the
card instances are those of a list child (id+"/list") whose rows are the
cards, so j/k, g g, ctrl+d/ctrl+u and 3j behave as on any page list; the
timeline draws the cards itself, in a window of its own measured in
lines (top, the first card drawn and how many of its lines are above
the window), and reports the selected card's lines with PlacedAt so the
page keeps it on screen.

Selection rule (the cards' exception to a page list's reverse video):
the selection is the border, and no text on a card is ever reversed.
The selected card of a focused timeline has a heavy border with heavy
corners in the accent, bold; the selected card of a timeline without
the focus a heavy border with rounded corners halfway from the accent
to the border role; every other card a light rounded border in the
border role faded by dim-unfocused (Styles.CardBorders). Header, meta
and body text keep their own styles whether selected or not. A
timeline without the focus is also faded as a whole (dim-unfocused),
as a page list is.

type Timeline struct {
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}

ToneSpan type
ToneSpan gives a run of one cell a tone. Cell is the column index (in a
list without columns, and a tree without them, 0 is the label); From and
To are rune offsets into the cell's text as drawn (escape sequences,
which a cell never draws, do not count), To 0 meaning the cell's end, so
{Cell, Tone} alone tones the whole cell. A later span wins where two
overlap; a span outside the text draws nothing. Bg names a tone (a theme
token) whose background goes under the span while Tone keeps the
foreground and attributes; Bg alone, with no Tone, only fills. Where
spans overlap, the foreground comes from the last span with a Tone and
the background from the last span that has one.

Layer names the highlight the span belongs to ("column", "row",
"search": any word the application picks); a span without one is a
plain tone. Highlights compose: where two or more layers cover a cell
(the selected row is the layer "selected", a non-data part such as the
header or a row-number column is a layer named after its part), the
cell takes the intersect tone of its part (list.cell.intersect for a
data cell, list.header.intersect, list.rownum.intersect), so a
crossing reads at a glance (setting list.intersect, default on). A
whole-cell layer span (From and To 0) fills the column's whole width,
not only its text.

type ToneSpan struct {
	Cell  int    `json:"cell"`
	Tone  string `json:"tone,omitempty"`
	Bg    string `json:"bg,omitempty"`
	From  int    `json:"from,omitempty"`
	To    int    `json:"to,omitempty"`
	Layer string `json:"layer,omitempty"`
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}
Source file
ToneStyle type
ToneStyle is how a scheme draws one tone: Fg is a colour as a scheme
role takes it ("#RRGGBB", "#RGB", an ANSI index, {"light", "dark"}) or
the name of a palette role (text, muted, accent, border, surface, error)
so the tone follows the scheme; empty keeps the text's colour. Bg is the
background, a colour or role the same way; empty draws none. Bold and
Dim (faint) are the attributes, the only part mono draws. In themes.json
a tone is {"fg": ..., "bg": ..., "bold": true, "dim": true}, or a colour
or role alone ("#5FAFD7", "error").

type ToneStyle struct {
	Fg   SchemeColor `json:"fg"`
	Bg   SchemeColor `json:"bg,omitzero"`
	Bold bool        `json:"bold,omitempty"`
	Dim  bool        `json:"dim,omitempty"`
}

)

Tree type
Tree is a list whose rows are nested nodes: expand and collapse, the
same motions as a list (it is one: rows spill to the neighbouring stops
in drawing order), the one highlighted row, columns like a table. Given
OnChange it is an outline editor: every structural edit is written at
once, typing within a line is written after a pause (Debounce) and when
the line is left, undo and redo are changes too, and esc never discards.

The tree is an instance of the outline noun with a list child
(id+"/list") holding the visible rows; editing adds an input
(id+"/line", noun outline-line) under the tree while a line is edited.
(The nouns are "outline", not "tree", so palette prefixes such as "tr"
stay unique in apps.)

type Tree struct {

	// Guides is the app's choice of branch lines (also set by Guides()).
	// tree.guides=off overrides it.
	Guides bool

	// OnChange makes the tree editable and receives each change as it is
	// made; an error keeps the change on screen, marks the row and retries
	// it before the next change.
	OnChange func(TreeChange) error
	// NewID names a new node (default "<tree>-n<seq>").
	NewID func() string
	// RowNoun is the noun of nodes the tree adds (default TreeRowNoun).
	RowNoun string
	// Refuse is the application's check on a change before it is made
	// (rename, move, delete and add; for add the node is the parent, the
	// zero node at the top): a non-nil error refuses it with that reason,
	// from every path. It runs after the nodes' Fixed. Undo and redo are
	// not checked: they take back changes that were allowed.
	Refuse func(node TreeNode, change TreeChange) error
	// Debounce is the pause in typing before a line is written.
	Debounce time.Duration
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}
Source file
Tree.Block function / method
Block renders the rows as the list does; while a line is open its row is
the input, with the terminal cursor in it. A zoomed tree draws its
breadcrumb line above the rows (tree.breadcrumb line) when there is room
for a row under it; the frame first applies tree.zoom (frameZoom).

func (t *Tree) Block(width, height int) Block

TreeAction type
TreeAction is an application change that goes on an outline's undo
stack in order with the tree's own edits: a change the application keeps
outside the tree's nodes, such as a task's status. u runs Undo and
ctrl+r runs Redo; each is written by the application like any change.
ID is the node selected after either runs ("" keeps the selection).

type TreeAction struct {
	ID    string
	Label string // what the change is ("done n1"), for logs
	Undo  func() error
	Redo  func() error
}

Source file
TreeChange type
TreeChange is one committed edit of an outline: add, rename, move or
delete. Index is the node's position among its parent's children after
the change. The application writes each one to its store as it comes
(Tree.OnChange); undo is a further change, never a discard.

type TreeChange struct {
	Op     string `json:"op"`
	ID     string `json:"id"`
	Parent string `json:"parent,omitempty"`
	Index  int    `json:"index"`
	Text   string `json:"text,omitempty"`
}

TreeChange.String function / method
String reads a change in one line: "add n1 under root at 1: text",
"rename n1: text", "move n1 under a at 0", "delete n1".

func (c TreeChange) String() string

Source file
TreeNode type
TreeNode is one node of a tree. Nodes are flat and in display order: a
node's children follow it, and Parent "" is a root. Noun is the row's
noun (default TreeRowNoun); Data, Cells and Tones are as on ListItem (cell
0 is the label: its tones stay on it, after the indent and fold marker).

type TreeNode struct {
	ID     string     `json:"id"`
	Parent string     `json:"parent,omitempty"`
	Label  string     `json:"label"`
	Noun   string     `json:"noun,omitempty"`
	Data   any        `json:"data,omitempty"`
	Props  Thing      `json:"props,omitempty"`
	Cells  []string   `json:"cells,omitempty"`
	Tones  []ToneSpan `json:"tones,omitempty"`
	// Section is the label line drawn above a run of rows in one section
	// (ListItem.Section); "" none.
	Section string `json:"section,omitempty"`
	// Search is more text the / filter matches the node by, never drawn
	// (ListItem.Search).
	Search []string `json:"search,omitempty"`
	// Fixed names the changes the node refuses: TreeRename, TreeMove
	// (move, indent, outdent) and TreeDelete (deleting it or any
	// ancestor). An outline's document root is Fixed all three; an array
	// item, whose key is its index, refuses rename alone. The refusal holds
	// from every path (keys, the palette, the CLI, Tree methods); the verb
	// is not available on the node (outline-renamable, outline-movable,
	// outline-deletable), so its hints and help rows drop out.
	Fixed []string `json:"fixed,omitempty"`
}

Refuses reports whether the node refuses the change op (TreeRename,

func (n TreeNode) Refuses(op string) bool

TreeZoomSetting, TreeZoomRootSetting, TreeBreadcrumbSetting, TreeBreadcrumbAncestorsSetting, TreeSpillSetting constant
The tree zoom settings, rows of settings/ochazuke.json.

const (
	// TreeZoomSetting is how a tree zooms: TreeZoomManual (the default)
	// only through the zoom verbs, TreeZoomAuto also when the selected
	// row's indent takes more than half the tree's width, TreeZoomOff
	// never (the verbs are unavailable and a zoom in place is dropped).
	TreeZoomSetting = "tree.zoom"
	// TreeZoomRootSetting is where the zoomed-on node goes:
	// TreeZoomRootCrumb (the default) ends the breadcrumb and its children
	// are the top rows; TreeZoomRootRow keeps it as the top row.
	TreeZoomRootSetting = "tree.zoom.root"
	// TreeBreadcrumbSetting is where a zoomed tree draws its breadcrumb:
	// TreeBreadcrumbLine (the default) a dimmed line above the rows,
	// TreeBreadcrumbTitle the header beside the mode (after the focus
	// display) while the tree holds the focus, TreeBreadcrumbOff nowhere.
	TreeBreadcrumbSetting = "tree.breadcrumb"
	// TreeBreadcrumbAncestorsSetting is how many of the nearest hidden
	// ancestors the breadcrumb names (default 2); farther ones fold into
	// "…". 0 names them all.
	TreeBreadcrumbAncestorsSetting = "tree.breadcrumb.ancestors"
	// TreeSpillSetting is which of a tree's h/l fall through to the list's
	// edge rows (scroll the columns, then move the focus to the previous
	// or next stop, R5.11) on a row where the tree's own verb has nothing
	// to do: TreeSpillLeft (the default) h only, TreeSpillBoth h and l (l
	// on a leaf), TreeSpillOff neither.
	TreeSpillSetting = "tree.spill"
)

UI type
UI is the component that routes handler messages to the host's sink. It
is one instance under app (Runtime.UI), never a Runtime field. With no
sink it discards, except that until the first sink attaches it keeps
the last 32 messages (the startup ring) and replays them, in order, into
that sink; after the first attach only the last nonempty message is
kept, bounded for the inspector.

Like the rest of Runtime it is called from the update loop; work off the
loop posts from its Then (which gets a Ctx).

type UI struct {
	// contains filtered or unexported fields
}

UI.Attach function / method
Attach makes s the sink every later message goes to and returns the one
it replaces (nil for none). The first attach of the runtime's life
replays the startup ring into s and drops it. Attach(nil) detaches:
messages are discarded from then on.

UIMessage type
UIMessage is one thing a handler told the user: a notice (Kind notice,
ID and Text), the clearing of one (Kind clear, ID), or the status line in
its error tone (Kind status, Text) or its neutral tone (Kind info, Text);
"" clears the line. The startup ring, a call's Response.Messages and
RecordingSink hold these.

type UIMessage struct {
	Kind string   `json:"kind"`
	ID   NoticeID `json:"id,omitempty"`
	Text string   `json:"text,omitempty"`
}

Source file
UINotice, UIClearNotice, UIError, UIInfo constant
UI message kinds. The status line is one line with two tones: UIError
is the error tone (Ctx.Error; the TUI draws "Error: TEXT" in the error
style, the CLI prints "error: TEXT") and UIInfo the neutral one
(Ctx.Info: plain text before the hints, "status: TEXT" on the CLI).
Either replaces the other. The kind strings are wire values
(Response.Messages), so UIError keeps its old wire value "status".

const (
	UINotice      = "notice"
	UIClearNotice = "clear"
	UIError       = "status"
	UIInfo        = "info"
)
Source file
UISink type
UISink is how a host shows what handlers tell the user: the Model puts
notices in the footer and the status in the status line, the CLI shell
prints them on stderr, a test records them. Handlers never reach the
sink: they post through Ctx (ctx.Notify, ctx.ClearNotice, ctx.Error,
ctx.Info) or Runtime.UI, and the host decides how it looks. Every sink
shows both tones of the status line, so a plain message is never drawn
as an error for want of a method. A sink that wraps another forwards
every message (UIMessage.Apply does it).

type UISink interface {
	// Notify shows a persistent notice until ClearNotice(id) or the host
	// clears it.
	Notify(id NoticeID, text string)
	// ClearNotice takes back a notice; an unknown id is ignored.
	ClearNotice(id NoticeID)
	// Status replaces the one-line status with an error ("" clears it):
	// what Ctx.Error posts (UIError). Only the error tone reaches it.
	Status(text string)
	// Info replaces the one-line status with plain text ("" clears it):
	// what Ctx.Info posts (UIInfo).
	Info(text string)
}
Source file
UIStatus constant
UIStatus is the error tone's message kind.

Deprecated: use UIError (the same wire value, "status").

const UIStatus = UIError

WatchEvent type
WatchEvent is one change to a watched thing: its type, its key (id, ""
for a singleton) and the thing as get answers it (every prop but the
secret ones). A deleted thing carries its last record when the watch saw
it, else none. Hash is the since a later watch resumes from: a fallback
watch's list hash after the change, or what a store's Watch sets.

type WatchEvent struct {
	Type  string         `json:"type"`
	ID    string         `json:"id"`
	Thing map[string]any `json:"thing,omitempty"`
	Hash  string         `json:"hash,omitempty"`
}
Source file
WatchQuery type
WatchQuery is what a store's Watch handler is asked for: one thing (Key)
or the things whose props equal Where (typed by their props; empty for
every thing), from Since ("" for now: the store chooses whether to send
the current things first).

type WatchQuery struct {
	Key   string
	Where map[string]any
	Since string
}

WatchVersions type
WatchVersions keeps the versions a fallback watch resumes from (since):
per scope (a noun and its query), a version's sorted ids and their record
hashes under the list hash. A version that is gone answers ok false.

type WatchVersions interface {
	ReadVersion(scope, hash string) (ids, hashes []string, ok bool)
	WriteVersion(scope, hash string, ids, hashes []string) error
}

Where function / method

Source file
WithInput function / method
WithInput places an input inside the modal body and focuses it, so a
one-field prompt is one call. The input is registered under the body (a
Kind option names its noun; Parent is ignored) and is removed with the
body on Pop; the *Input keeps its text and undo history and may be
passed again to the next OpenModal.

func WithInput(in *Input, opts ...InstanceOption) ModalOption

Yank type
Yank retains the geometry needed to paste independently of the source field.

type Yank struct {
	Text  string `json:"text"`
	Shape string `json:"shape"`
	Width int    `json:"width,omitempty"`
}

YankReportSetting constant
YankReportSetting names the setting that has an editor's yank say what
it copied on the status line (yankReport).

const YankReportSetting = "yank.report"
